## Global decisions

- APR-002 part 1 (2026-11-07): approval links (`features/approval/links.ts`, server only) are HMAC-SHA256 signed claims holding ids only: `v`, `tenant`, `sub` (the owner the email goes to), `batch`, `act` (`approve_all` with the shown emails, or `change` with exactly one), `items` as `[campaignId, revision]` pairs, a link id `jti` and an expiry 7 days out; one strict zod schema checks them on both sign and verify, and only the canonical signature spelling is accepted. Secrets are 32+ characters; the first signs, any in the list verifies (rotation). Who may use a link: holding it is the recipient owner's authority, but only while it still holds; at the button the server re-checks from membership, never from the claim, that `sub` is still an active owner of `tenant`, and a signed-in user must be `sub` (`other_user` otherwise). When a link is used up: opening it (GET) only calls `verifyApprovalLink` and shows a confirmation page, so mail scanners never approve or use it up; the button (POST) calls `redeemApprovalLink`, whose `ApprovalLinkStore.redeem` is one database command that locks the workspace, re-checks the owner, records `jti` once (unique key) and applies the action to exactly `items` in the same transaction, answering `used`, `not_owner` or `changed` (PT409 when a revision moved); nothing is recorded unless the action applies. Approve all from the email approves only the emails the email showed, at their revisions (APR-001 `approveAll(items, shown, …)`). `weeklyApprovalEmail` (`email.ts`) lists only emails still open (waiting and at least an hour ahead) soonest first, with date in the store timezone, offer, audience, phone screenshot and warnings; it signs the Approve all link for exactly the shown emails and each Change link once, refuses to build when nothing is waiting, escapes every word, keeps the subject on one line, accepts only clean https links, has a preheader, says when the links stop working, and does not invite replies until replies are read.

- APR-001 part 1 (2026-11-07): the weekly approval rules (`features/approval/weekly.ts`). A batch item carries its copy/design `revision`, send time, subject, offer, segment, state (`pending_approval`, `change_requested`, `needs_human`, `approved`), change rounds and whether the owner asked for a change. Every approval is bound to what the owner saw: `approveAll(items, shown, context, tenantId, now)` approves only the `{ campaignId, revision }` pairs shown, still waiting and at least `minimumLeadMinutes` (60) before the send, and reports every other email with a `SkipReason` (`skipWords` has the owner's words, with no product name); the store re-checks the revisions in the same transaction and raises PT409 when one moved. `requestChange(item, shownRevision, …)` sends a change to the AI at most twice (`maxChangeRounds`), the third to a person (VISION rule 13); `backForApproval` brings a redo or a person's finished version back as a new revision. The owner check is the shared `can(context, 'approve', tenantId)` (owner of that workspace only), never a second rule. `approvedWithoutEdits` is the share VISION tracks from the first client.

- ASM-003 (2026-11-07): `assembleFromLayout(input, brand, run, mode, layout, feedback)` (`features/assembly/modes.ts`) builds the client's email from a competitor layout (`layoutSchema`: the labelled `sections`, top to bottom, and an optional `angle`, checked on arrival). The angle is untrusted: a labeller wrote it after reading the competitor's email, so it never appears in the client's email in any mode, and it reaches the writer only as `strategyNote` inside the JSON data, with a rule that it describes structure only and that no name, number, claim, offer or phrase may be used from it. Exact keeps the sequence with the client's own content and words from the brief (the angle is not sent); Rebrand keeps the sequence and the angle's structure; Adapt passes the angle to ASM-001's planner (`assembleEmail(…, { strategyNote })`). `layoutSequence(layout, allowed, products)` keeps the order, budgets products the way `fill` takes them (so a 3-column grid after a hero may become 2 columns), gives a missing section the nearest block the client can fill (a stand-in, with a plain reason) and returns every section it left out (`dropped`: unsure, not a block type, over the 12-block cap). The fixed-sequence request lists each block with its word limits, tells the writer what a stand-in must not write about (no founder story for a founder note, no reviews for a review strip), carries ASM-001's `writerRules`, and sends `offer: null` when the layout has no offer section; a live answer must have every block in order, else `invalid_output` with feedback. Without a live writer, steps and FAQ sections become text and the sample words come only from the campaign type, brand and catalog. A layout with fewer than 3 usable blocks, or only header, announcement and footer, is refused. It returns ASM-001's fail-closed result (`ready`, `rewrite` or `sample`, with the same engine checks through the shared `finish`; only `ready` may go on, `checkAssembled`) plus `mode`, `layout`, `dropped` and `notes` for the owner (sections left out, no offer section for the client's code, no room for the brief's product in the layout, no angle found). Because the writer cannot add a product block to a fixed layout, a focus product the layout has no room for is a note, not a problem.

- ASM-001 part 1 (2026-11-07): `assembleEmail(input, brand, run, { feedback?, strategyNote? })` (`features/assembly/assemble.ts`) turns a brief into checked blocks. `AssemblyInput` carries the brief, the cleaned catalog, the client's codes, `maxDiscountPercent`, `sendAt`, the store `timezone`, the `facts` sheet and confirmed material; `DiscountCode` (`expiresAt: string | null`) and `BrandFacts` have exactly copy QA's shapes (ASM-002, #323), and part 2 imports them from `features/copy-qa` once it merges; `assemblyInputSchema` checks stored input at the job boundary (ISO times with an offset, a known IANA timezone); an unknown timezone also stops `assembleEmail`. The AI writes words only: products, prices, pictures, links, the offer's percent, the code and its end date (the last minute, in the store's timezone) are placed by code, and founder notes, review strips and customer photos only ever carry the client's own words (the model writes nothing for them). `allowedBlockTypes` offers a block only when material that fits it exists (`usableMaterial`): grids need enough products (at most 12 are used, `maxProducts`), `offer_banner` a listed code within the max that still works at `sendAt`, `review_strip` confirmed reviews that pass the block and a verified review source (`facts.reviewsVerified`), `founder_note` the founder's own note, `faq` a known fact, and buttons, banners and the photo hero a link (the store or a product). A product shows its old price only when the cleaning step marked it a temporary sale (`compareAtIsSale`) and the implied discount is within the max (Step 0 finding 2). Products never repeat. A focus product that cannot be placed stops the build (`AssemblyError` `focus_unavailable`, for a person). The writer gets the allowed blocks with their word limits, shared `writerRules` (no price, code or link; only the offer's own percent; shipping, delivery, returns and guarantees only from the facts; no results, social proof, founder story or false urgency), and the store's data as one JSON line marked as untrusted, with the last failed checks inside it; thinking is set to low with 4,000 output tokens. `assembleEmail` fails closed: it returns `Assembled`, either `{ status: 'ready', draft }`, `{ status: 'rewrite', draft, problems }` or `{ status: 'sample', draft }`, and only `ready` may go on; `checkAssembled(result)` is the one check part 2 merges with copy QA's in `withCopyQa`, and it never passes a rewrite or a sample. A result is `rewrite` when the engine finds anything wrong with what the email will print (the clipped words of the kept blocks, normalised with NFKC and without invisible characters): blocks not allowed, words over a limit, too few products left, the brief's product not shown, a product named that the email does not show, a percent (written `%`, `percent` or `per cent`) other than the placed offer's, or any percent without an offer block, shipping, delivery, returns or guarantee words that are not a fact line word for word (whether or not the fact is known), results or timings, social proof, a founder story, banned phrases. Only the titles of the products shown, the approved claims and the fact lines are masked first; questions are not read as claims. The order is put right without a retry: at most one announcement bar first, then the header, then the content (one offer, cut to the 12-block cap, never the header), then one footer; an answer with nothing usable is `AiRunError('invalid_output')` with feedback; provider failures keep their wait and diagnostic. With the AI port's fake the sample writer builds the email from the campaign type, brand name and catalog titles only (never the brief), never has problems, and says `source: 'sample'`; part 2 never sends a sample draft for approval. The draft lists the placed `productIds` and the `code`. `copyDraftOf(draft)` gives `checkCopy` the subject, preview and each block's words with prices, old prices and the code line.

- RET-001 (2026-11-07): decision D3, owner's smallest-change order. `proxy.ts` answers every `/designer…` route and `/admin/designers` with the app's Not found page and a real 404 (`features/designers/retired-paths.ts`), signed in or not, and the admin sidebar has no Designers item. The designer code, tables and status words stay untouched; removing "With designer" from the status system and closing old assignments is left for later.

- BLK-003 part 1 (2026-11-07): `labelSection({ html, text?, image? }, run)` (`features/blocks/labeller.ts`) returns `{ type: BlockType | 'unsure', confidence, source: 'gemini' | 'sample', model, promptVersion, note? }` for one competitor section (`source` matches INB-003's `label_source`; `labelPromptVersion` is bumped whenever the prompt or rules change). `run` has `runAi`'s answer shape (GEM-001, #322): the caller passes `(request) => runAi({ tenantId, callerKey: 'section:<id>', purpose: 'label', request }, { ledger })`. A section over `sectionLimits` (50,000 characters or 2,000 tags) is not parsed and comes back `unsure` with `note: 'too_large'`, without a call. Otherwise the HTML is parsed once; counts and visible text come from that one tree (scripts, styles, templates, comments and hidden elements skipped; each cell and paragraph on its own line; cut without splitting an emoji). The request sends the counts first and the text as a JSON string marked as data, never instructions, with a fixed-list JSON schema (the 20 keys plus `unsure`), temperature 0 and `reasoningEffort: 'none'`. An answer that is not exactly the schema is `invalid_output`; under 0.6 confidence the type becomes `unsure`, for the ops queue. A refusal becomes `unsure` with `note: 'refused'`; other failures become `LabelError(code, retryAfterSeconds)` with `retryable` true only for rate limits, outages, timeouts, incomplete or invalid answers and a busy ledger. When `runAi` answers with the fake, the sample rules answer instead. Pictures are not sent: the AI port has no image input (GEM-001 does not add one), so the gate runs on text. `scoreLabels(pairs)` (`label-score.ts`) is the Step 1 gate: at least 50 different hand-labelled sections, at least 85% agreement (shown rounded down), only labels from the live model (`source: 'gemini'`), `unsure` counts as a miss, with the misses and a confusion table; invalid labels are refused.

- BLK-001 (2026-11-07): the block library is the one way emails are built (VISION Step 1, decision D3), with the Step 0 renderer (`docs/step0/render.py`) as the reference. `features/blocks/catalog.ts` fixes the 20 types (no countdown): the 10 Step 0 types keep their keys and input fields (`announcement_bar`, `header_logo`, `hero_product`, `text`, `steps`, `product_grid_2col`, `faq`, `cta`, `signoff`, `footer`; `parseSpecBlock` reads a Step 0 spec block as it is), and 10 more follow the same conventions (`image`+`alt`, `cta`+`url`, `heading`). Every content schema is strict; links must be https/http/mailto without credentials and pictures https (or inline PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP for previews), at most 2000 characters. `BlockBrand` carries the Step 0 roles (`name`, `site`, `logo`, `font`, `ink`, `background`, `accent`, `dark`, `radius`); `brandFromKit` picks roles from an unordered `brand_kits.colors` list by lightness and chroma. `renderEmail` re-validates every block (a stored block can never skip the schema), draws a white 600px container on the background with `bgcolor` for Outlook, stacks under 620px, gives one h1, ends with exactly one footer, and in `klaviyo` mode refuses inline pictures and writes the Step 0 footer tags (`{{ organization.name }}`, `{{ organization.full_address }}`, `{% unsubscribe "Unsubscribe" %}`, `{% manage_preferences "Preferences" %}`, `{% web_view "View in browser" %}`). All text is escaped including `{`, `}` and bidi controls.

- Ready buttons (2026-11-07): `SubmitButton` and the `ConfirmDialog` trigger stay disabled until the page is interactive (`components/ui/use-hydrated.ts`, `useSyncExternalStore`: false in the server HTML and during hydration, true in the browser). A click on a slow page before hydration used to post the form natively, so the page reloaded and its "saved" message was lost, or the dialog never opened. This was the first failure behind the mobile UC-09/12/16 browser runs. Playwright waits for a button to be enabled before it clicks, so the journeys no longer race hydration. Unit tests render on the client and are unaffected.

- CI browser timeout (2026-11-07): the UC-09 scheduling journey (about 40 server actions) gets 300s on CI instead of 150s; a run ran out of time at its last reload. On CI, Playwright `expect()` checks wait up to 15s instead of 5s (`playwright.config.ts`; locally still 5s). Since the Connections, Brand, Profile and Dashboard journeys landed, a Browser run takes 20–30 minutes on one dev server, and the last mobile journeys (UC-09, UC-12, UC-16) timed out at whichever server action was slowest, on every pull request. Checks must still come true; they only get more time. Side finding for a follow-up: `finish_onboarding_job` and `private.onboarding_lease` raise SQLSTATE 40001 ('Connection changed', 'Lease unavailable'), the PT409 trap in CLAUDE.md; a stale `commerce.sync` (queued, then the Shopify revision bumped) hits it. That needs a new migration.

- UIR-025 (2026-11-07): designers see an assignment in their own words: **To do** (assigned), **Redo requested** (revisions requested), **Waiting for owner** (submitted), **Approved**, and **Brief changed** on the brief page when the approved copy moved on (stale; the desk list shows the stored state). The owner keeps the UIR-015 campaign words (With designer, Needs your approval). Designer due times show the exact UTC time next to a relative line ("Due in 3 hours · Nov 7, 2026 · 3:00 PM UTC"; "Was due 2 hours ago" in red only while it is the designer's turn), because one designer works across brands in different timezones. The words live in `features/designers/model.ts`.

- UIR-005 (2026-11-06): Brand Truth and Copy & Voice are one editable **Brand profile** (OWNER_JOURNEYS §18 decision 5). Brand facts live in one place: brand name, website (`domain`), positioning, Do (`voice`) and Don’t (`prohibitions`) stay only in `brand_profiles`, which every AI feature reads after the owner approves; the Identity and Tone cards show them from there and save them with a brand-revision check (`PT409` "Brand details changed in another window" if Get started or another tab changed them), so no copy can drift or overwrite newer facts. Section notes (niche, market, story, audience, tone tiles, products, vocabulary, sample copy, audience persona) live in `brand_profile_sections` (members read via RLS; field keys and limits defined in SQL `private.brand_section_fields`, mirrored by `features/brand-profile/sections.ts`, checked by a test against the newest migration). The AI does not read these notes yet, and the cards say so; they have no approval binding. Owner-only `save_brand_section(t, section, expected_revision, content, locked, facts, expected_brand_revision)` returns `{revision, brand_changed}`; a real fact change bumps the brand revision, clears the approval and (existing `brand_guard`) resets unsent campaigns or refuses during delivery; unchanged facts (outer spaces, blank lines, CRLF ignored) change nothing. `apply_brand_suggestion` ("Redo with AI") merges onto the current content: suggested unlocked fields only, omitted and locked fields kept, brand facts impossible. The domain-local `BrandProfileWriter` port has only `SampleBrandProfileWriter`: it fills empty fields with text marked "Sample: …" and never replaces owner text or guesses tone tiles or a hero product; any `AI_DEFAULT_PROVIDER` other than `fake` refuses. Brand-mismatch guard: `brandMismatches` compares the brand name (website only when the name has nothing distinctive) with the Klaviyo account label and the Shopify handle (whole-word subset, run-together names, initials) and shows a warning banner (the owner can ignore it when both are right). Catalogue, offers and brand kit moved unchanged into `CataloguePanels`. `/brand/voice` redirects to `/brand#voice`; `/store` keeps the sub-tabs and highlights Brand & Store in the sidebar (`parentAreas`, one line in the shell's `nav-data.ts`); `proxy.ts` now refreshes sessions on `/store`.

- UIR-019 (2026-11-06): `/team` follows OWNER_JOURNEYS §14. The stored role `admin` is shown as **Manager** everywhere on the page (no enum rename; `features/team/rules.ts` `roleLabel`). Owners and managers manage the team; staff see it read-only. New security-definer commands: `team_members(t)` (active members only; returns the sign-in email from `auth.users` because profiles keep none), `resend_team_invite(t, invite)` (7 more days; PT409 when cancelled or accepted), `change_team_member(t, membership, expected_at, role, status)` (role `admin`/`staff`, status `active`/`inactive`; refuses the owner row and your own access with 42501, stale pages with PT409). `invite_team` now refuses an active member's address and a second open invite (PT409 "Already a member"/"Already invited") and cancels an expired invite to the same address; `revoke_team_invite` says PT409 when already gone. **Members no longer write `tenant_memberships` or `tenant_invites` directly** (insert/update/delete revoked from `authenticated`; RLS write policies stay but have no grant), and `update_team_access` is no longer granted to members (it could change owner rows; nothing calls it). IAM's last-owner trigger is unchanged; its pgTAP case now runs as the database owner. Email delivery stays simulated and the page says so; invited people sign up with that address and accept on `/onboarding`. Review fixes in `20260914090025`: an address that is a deactivated member is refused (PT409 "Deactivated member": use Reactivate); `accept_team_invite` (IAM's command, narrowly changed) applies the invited role, never changes an owner row, and refuses a deactivated membership (PT409); every team write goes through `private.require_team_manager(t)` (check, lock the tenant, check again); `team_members` returns deactivated rows to owners and managers only; cancelling an unknown or other workspace's invite is 42501 "Invite unavailable"; changes need an active or inactive member; expired invites cancelled on re-invite are logged; `update_team_access` is closed to the service role too. The page reads `workspace_events` (membership and invitation kinds, newest 20) for **Recent team changes**, which replaces the old panel's access audit.

- UIR-020 (2026-11-06): `/feedback` follows OWNER_JOURNEYS §15 with §18.16 wording ("New request" / "Send"). A feature request is a `support_tickets` row of kind `feedback` with the new nullable `category` ('feature', 'improvement', 'bug', 'general'; a check allows it on feedback only), written by the member-only `request_feature(t, category, subject, details)` (subject 2–200 characters on one line, details 1–5000 after trimming, logged as `feature_requested`). The owner's status comes from the existing ticket state set by the platform team in the admin support queue: open = Open, in_progress = Planned, closed = Done; the first message is the details, later messages are replies, and a reply from someone outside the workspace is labelled "<product name> team". Members hold only select on `support_tickets` and `support_messages` (anon none; RLS already blocked row writes, but truncate and trigger are not covered by RLS). Review fixes in `20260914090027`: `reply_support` refuses a member changing the state of a feedback ticket (42501; only operators set Open/Planned/Done; members can still reply), `open_support_ticket` gives feedback from the old Support form the type `general`, and `request_feature` also refuses blank-looking subjects and C1 control characters with an explicit range (locale-independent). The Support page still lists both kinds until UIR-010.

- UIR-023 (2026-11-07): one brand timezone everywhere (OWNER*JOURNEYS §18.14). `tenants.timezone` stays the ONE brand timezone (every page already gets it as `ctx.timezone`); `tenants.timezone_source` records 'setup', 'klaviyo' or 'owner'. `workspace_connections.account_timezone` keeps the Klaviyo account's zone (a timezone-name shape; see the review fixes below). `connect_sample_provider` gains `account_timezone` (the six-argument form is replaced; named calls without it still work) and sets the brand timezone from Klaviyo unless the owner chose one. Owner-only `set_brand_timezone(t, timezone, expected)` (PT409 if the page is stale) and "Use Klaviyo's timezone" (final form below); both log `brand_timezone_set`. The sample verifier reports the workspace timezone, so connecting does not move existing workspaces. Shared helpers in `lib/brand-time.ts` (additive, browser lane's file): `timezoneWithOffset(tz, at)` "Asia/Kathmandu (UTC+05:45)", `formatBrandDateTimeWithZone(value, tz)` "Nov 6, 2026 · 10:49 PM (Kathmandu time)", `formatBrandTimeWithZone(value, tz)` "10:49 PM Kathmandu time", `isKnownTimezone`, `timezoneOptions(current)` (always includes the workspace's own zone, because runtimes may name it differently, e.g. `Asia/Katmandu`). The browser lane adopts them in the planner, list, detail pages and schedule step. Review fixes (`20260914090029_brand_timezone_checks.sql`): nobody writes `tenants.timezone` directly any more (the column grant to `authenticated` is revoked; only the commands change it, with the log and the stale check); while a `calendar.plan` job is queued, leased, running or waiting to retry, `set_brand_timezone` and `use_klaviyo_timezone` refuse with PT409 'GROS is making a plan' and connecting Klaviyo keeps the current zone (the planner reads the zone when it starts); a Klaviyo zone Postgres does not know is stored as nothing ('not reported') instead of refusing the connection; saving the zone the brand already has changes nothing; only a real zone change is logged; `use_klaviyo_timezone(t, expected_zone)` replaces the one-argument form and refuses with PT409 'Klaviyo reported another timezone' when the page is stale; the account-timezone column accepts every real zone name (EST5EDT, GB-Eire, Etc/GMT+5) and refuses key-shaped values (`pk*`, `sk*`, `shpat*`, `shpca*`, `shpss*`, `shppa\_`). Settings sends back the zone exactly as saved (not the UTC fallback), the picker list is built on the server (no browser/server mismatch) and lists one spelling per zone (`sameTimezone`, new in `lib/brand-time.ts`). Second review (`20260914090030_brand_timezone_answers.sql`): `set_brand_timezone`returns whether it changed anything (false when the brand already has that zone, only after the stale-page check), so Settings never says "Nothing changed" to a stale page; the final switch is`use_klaviyo_timezone(t, expected_zone, expected)`(Klaviyo's zone and the brand zone the page showed; PT409 'The timezone changed in another window' when either is stale); switching only where the zone came from (the brand already has Klaviyo's zone) is allowed during a plan; every change is logged, also a change of only the source, with`from_source`. An owner cannot turn a Klaviyo zone into "Chosen by you" by saving the same zone (saving the same zone changes nothing); they pick another zone. Campaigns already planned keep their own `campaigns.timezone` and send moment. Daily AI usage and digest days are counted in the brand timezone, so on the day it changes that day can cover more or less than 24 hours.
- UIR-021 (2026-11-07): brand-mismatch guard (OWNER_JOURNEYS §0.1, §18.6). A connected account now records what business it belongs to: `workspace_connections.account_website` (Klaviyo; the origin only, `https://host`, since 090032), `account_currency` (ISO 4217) and `account_country` (ISO 3166 alpha-2), all shape-checked so no key fits; a fact in another shape is stored as nothing ("not reported") and never refuses the connection. `connect_sample_provider` gains three optional arguments (the seven-argument form is replaced). The sample verifier reports the workspace's brand name, no website, USD, US and the workspace's own timezone; a sample Klaviyo key ending in `0000` reports ANOTHER brand's account ("Northwind Outdoor (sample)", https://northwind-outdoor.example, EUR, DE) but still the workspace's timezone, so trying it never moves the brand timezone. A Shopify token ending in `0000` reports EUR and DE, which only shows a warning while Klaviyo is connected too. `brandMismatches` compares the Brand profile with Klaviyo (name, website), Klaviyo with Shopify (currency, country) and the catalogue (more than half of the product links on another site, only when the profile has a website). Countries are named in words ("Germany"). The Brand page and `brandGuard` keep the name and store checks on a database without the facts columns. The Brand page banner shows every difference; `<BrandCheck tenantId action>` (server component) shows the first one with a link to /brand before AI steps and never blocks. Shown on /gros ("before GROS answers") and /campaign-agent ("before GROS plans or writes your campaigns"). Still to place: the campaign detail page's copy step (`app/(client)/campaigns/[campaignId]`, open in the browser lane's #284) and Create email (`app/(auth)/create-email`); one `<BrandCheck tenantId />` line each.
- Vercel deploys (2026-11-07, owner request): `vercel.json` `git.deploymentEnabled` is `{ "main": true, "claude/**": false }`, so only `main` deploys; agent branches make no preview deployments (the free plan allows 100 deployments a day and every branch push made one). Preview checks of a branch now happen locally and in CI, not on Vercel. Agents batch commits and push once per PR when it is ready for CI.
- UIR-013 (2026-11-06): `/connections` follows `docs/UI_REBUILD_SPEC.md` §13 (two cards, Klaviyo key connect/swap, Shopify store form). Connections are still sample (fake-adapter) connections: a domain-local `ConnectionVerifier` port has only `SampleConnectionVerifier` (no network, never receives the secret); `ADAPTER_MODE` other than `fake` refuses to connect. The full key or token is never stored, logged or returned; `workspace_connections` gains display-only `account_label`, `credential_hint` (masked: prefix, eight bullets, last four; enforced by a check), `shop_domain` (`.myshopify.com` only), `template_count` and `connected_at`, written only through the owner-only `connect_sample_provider` (workspace lock first). The action falls back to `set_fixture_connection` if the hosted database lacks the migration. Spec text "validated against Klaviyo" is shown as "checked" until live verification exists. The old fixture status forms, Drive folder, readiness and setup jobs moved unchanged into a collapsed "Sample data tools" section (STAB-002 will move developer controls to admins). Migration numbered `20260914090017` in the integration range so it applies after 90016 on the hosted project.

- STAB-004 (2026-11-06): CI is split into three workflows. `ci.yml` (`verify`: format, lint, typecheck, unit tests, build) runs on every pull request and push to `main` and is the only check to require on `main`. `database.yml` (pgTAP) and `browser.yml` (Playwright plus the first-draft and email concurrency scripts) run on pull requests only when their files change, skip drafts, can be run by hand, and no longer re-run on push to `main`; both start a lean local Supabase (`-x studio,logflare,vector,imgproxy,edge-runtime,realtime,postgres-meta,supavisor`). Reason: the repository is private, so every job costs Actions minutes (GitHub rounds up per job); the full browser job is about 25 minutes. Path-filtered workflows must not be required checks, or pull requests that do not touch them would wait forever. Owner decision (2026-11-06): the scheduled-jobs workflow runs every 30 minutes (`3,33 * * * *`, about 1,500 Actions minutes a month), replacing the 10-minute schedule recorded under STAB-001.

- UIR-002 part 1 (2026-11-06): §0 layout facts of `docs/UI_REBUILD_SPEC.md` are now tokens: `radius-panel` 20px (cards, modals), `radius-control` 12px (inputs, buttons), `radius-small` 8px, base text 17px/1.5, H1 30px (serif kept at weight 400 because fonts stay from the design language), field labels 11px/600 uppercase mono, inputs 14px, `sidebar-width` 255px, `sidebar-rail` 56px, `detail-max` 1020px, `icon-size` 20px, plus an `accent-2` colour role (TEXT pills, cultural holidays) with AA checks. Tailwind `rounded-*` now maps sm→8px, md/lg→12px, xl+→20px. `docs/DESIGN.md` records that §0 wins.

- M1.5 (2026-11-06): on Raman's instruction the old app is rebuilt 1:1 from `docs/UI_REBUILD_SPEC.md` before M2. New milestone "M1.5 Old-app UI rebuild" in `.github/project/roadmap.json` with work items UIR-002…UIR-014 (GitHub #238–#250, one per spec section). Lanes: browser §2, §3, §4, §6, §7; cloud §5, §8–§14. Branding stays from the design tokens; §0 layout facts (20px cards, 12px controls, sizes, 255/56px sidebar, outlined icons) override `docs/DESIGN.md` where they differ. DS-007 is parked. Each PR ticks its spec checkboxes and fixes the §15 bugs.
- UIR-009 + UIR-022 (2026-11-07): the sidebar's **Inspiration** is `/library/inspirations` (`/competitor-agent` stays, as its advanced page, and highlights Inspiration). A workspace tracks at most **10** competitor domains: `subscribe_competitor` and `update_competitor_subscription` (turning a stopped domain back on) refuse an 11th with PT409 'You can track up to 10 domains' after `require_member` has locked the workspace (`private.competitor_slots_full`, 090034); re-tracking one it has does not count twice. `subscribe_competitor`'s label and rules may be null (since 090034): a new domain is labelled with itself, a stopped one keeps its label and private rules, and an already-tracked one is left untouched, so a playbook built on it stays valid. Tracking from the page uses the domain as the label and queues a `competitors.capture` job (the scheduled worker runs it); Remove is `update_competitor_subscription(active=false)` with the subscription's own private rules kept. The **reference library** is one component, `features/library/reference-library/reference-library.tsx` (client) fed by `referenceLibrary(tenantId)` (server, the user's own session, RLS applies): tabs `competitors` (the latest 20 emails of each tracked domain), `yours` (pasted or uploaded references and saved templates) and `best` (none yet; curated by the platform team), counts from `tabCounts`, search over title, sender, domain and text, SHOW All / Email Design / Email Text (`classifyFormat`: design when there are at most 60 words per image), domain chips, 20 per "Load more", sandboxed previews (`previewHtml` on the server, links removed; `ScaledFrame` with `sandbox=""`) and an in-page preview modal. `mode="browse"` links "Use this design" to `/first-draft?reference=…` or `?imported=…` (`referenceHref`); `mode="pick"` calls `onPick(item)` so New draft, the editor's design picker and the planner's "Send on which day?" dialog reuse it. Items carry `sample` and show a "Sample" pill (competitor emails and saved layouts are all samples in this version). The loader reads only what it shows: the latest 20 emails per tracked domain (one query each) and the latest 40 uploads. `SafePreview` is made only on the server (`toSafePreview` or `EmailPreviewCard`); a picker sends back only `item.id` and `item.source`. `canUse={false}` (staff) hides "Use this design" and the upload link; `mode="pick"` requires `onPick`, accepts `selected` (`{ id, source }`; a "Selected" pill) and closes the preview after a pick. When the latest 40 uploads were read, the Yours tab says "Showing your latest 40 uploads." Saved layouts show the label "Saved layout" instead of "Plain-text email" (`PlainTextEmail` `heading`, `PreviewCardFrame` `textHeading`).
- INB-001 (2026-11-07): the Inspiration inbox reads real competitor newsletters (VISION Step 2, BUILD_BRIEF D4). **Shared contracts:** `platform_jobs` (leased, retryable jobs that belong to no workspace; kinds `inbox.poll`, `inbox.assets`, `inbox.screenshot`, `inbox.label`, `inbox.confirm`; one active job per kind and subject; service-role RPCs `claim_platform_job(kinds, subject)`, `finish_platform_job`, `fail_platform_job`, lease 120 s, 4 attempts with backoff, then an ops task) and `ops_tasks` (work for a person: one open task per `dedupe_key`, repeats count up; kinds `inbox_unknown_sender`, `inbox_parse_failure`, `inbox_too_large`, `inbox_opt_in`, `inbox_unsubscribed`, `inbox_pending_subscription`, `inbox_auth_failed`, `job_failed`). Both are readable by platform admins only. The port is `lib/providers/inbound-capture.ts` (`InboundCaptureTransport`: `list` above an IMAP cursor, `fetchRaw` with a size cap, `markProcessed` = \\Seen only; never delete, move or expunge); `features/references/inbound.ts` keeps its older fixture-envelope interface of the same name. The fake (`features/inbox/fake-transport.ts`, sample `.example` brands) is the default; Gmail (`features/inbox/imap.ts`, imapflow 1.7.8 pinned, TLS 1.2+, no logger) is read when both `INBOX_IMAP_USER` and `INBOX_IMAP_APP_PASSWORD` are set in the server environment (checked, never logged); against a local database (a developer machine or a Claude session, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` on 127.0.0.1/localhost) Gmail also needs `INBOX_TRANSPORT=gmail_imap`, so copied production values never read the real inbox by accident; `INBOX_TRANSPORT=fake` always forces the sample inbox. **Because both values are already in Vercel, merging INB-001 starts reading the real Gmail inbox in production at the next scheduled sweep** (owner's go-ahead, 7 Nov). **The inbox does not follow `ADAPTER_MODE`:** the jobs route only runs workspace jobs with sample providers, but it reads the configured inbox in every mode. The scheduled sweep (`/api/internal/jobs`) polls the inbox first (at most 15 s; a slower poll finishes on its own under its lease), then runs workspace jobs in what is left of the 40 s budget; an inbox failure never stops them. **Sample and real never mix:** a brand is a sample exactly when its domain ends in `.example` (a check constraint), the fake mailbox reaches only sample brands and Gmail only real ones, and a real `competitor_emails` row always points at its inbox message. When a brand has registered sender domains, an email to its `+alias` from any other sender goes to a person (the alias address is easy to guess). Each message is stored once (raw `.eml` and its HTML in the private `inbox-raw` bucket under `<mailbox>/<sha256>`), keyed by its Message-ID (or bytes), and assigned by its `+alias` slug, then a registered sender domain (or its parent), else an ops task; the cursor moves after every message. `competitor_captures` are now brands (`alias_slug`, `brand_name`, `niche`, `subscription_status`; real domains allowed; registered by admins with `admin_save_inbox_brand`); workspaces still follow sample domains only. Assigned messages become real `competitor_emails` (`synthetic=false`, escaped-text preview, `micro` empty until INB-003 labels them); readers that need sample facts skip unlabelled rows. A failed Gmail login pauses the mailbox for 6 hours (so wrong logins never lock the account) and opens a task.
- INB-002 (2026-11-07): every image of an inbox email is kept as soon as it is recorded, and a 600px screenshot is taken. **Images:** recording an email with HTML queues an `inbox.assets` platform job (a database trigger). The sweep's inbox step runs these jobs after the poll, until 15 s into the step (the whole step stops waiting at 18 s). Each image the HTML uses is fetched once: `<img src>`, `srcset`, `background`, CSS `url()`, `cid:` parts and `data:` images, at most 60. Each is checked by its bytes (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP only; never SVG) and stored once by content as `img/<sha256>.<ext>` in the private `inbox-assets` bucket. A failure is recorded as `broken`, `too_large` (5 MiB per image, 25 MiB per email), `not_image`, `blocked` or `timeout`. The live fetcher (`createImageFetcher`) opens only http(s) on ports 80/443, never a private, local or reserved address (checked on the DNS answer itself and again on every redirect, at most 3), with no cookies or credentials. Sample emails use a sample fetcher with no network. **Screenshot:** a server function cannot run a browser, so a scheduled GitHub Actions job (`inbox-screenshots.yml`, minutes 18 and 48, same `CRON_SECRET` and `SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED` switch as the sweep) calls `GET /api/internal/inbox/screenshots`. With `?peek=1` that only counts what is waiting; without it, it leases up to 4 jobs. Each job carries only short-lived signed links: the email HTML, its stored images and a one-time upload link for `shot/<message id>.jpg`. The runner renders at 600px with JavaScript off, blocks every request outside those links, uploads a full-height JPEG (capped at 16,000px) and reports the size with `POST`. A retry overwrites the same picture. **Who sees what:** the screenshot path is copied to `competitor_emails`. A storage policy (`private.inbox_asset_access`) lets a member read a screenshot or image only when their workspace follows the brand of an email that uses it; platform admins read all. Inspiration cards show the screenshot when there is one.
- CI-001 (2026-11-11): **competitor intelligence is multi-label** (AGENT_MAP §1, D18). Every competitor email gets one value per email label (industry, email type, offer type, season, angle, brand size, from fixed lists in `features/intelligence/taxonomy.ts`; the database checks the same lists and a test compares them), and every section a family (the 20 seed block types are the 20 families, still stored in `email_sections.block_type`, which lane A's copy mode reads), a layout, a purpose and up to three styles. Two agents, as MEM-001 `AgentDefinition`s run by `runAgent` with `tenantId: null` (platform work on the platform's daily budget, GEM-003): the **Section tagger** (`section-tagger`) with the section's crop and the **Email classifier** (`email-classifier`) with the screenshot, both on purpose `label` (Flash-Lite). An answer that is unreadable or below 0.6 is asked again with the per-call purpose `review` (Flash); still unsure, or refused, it is stored `untagged` (no labels, left out of search, no person asked, D16). With no live model set up the honest sample rules answer (`pass: 'sample'`): the INB-003 family rules, a layout from the section's table structure, the family's usual purpose, styles from words and pictures, the industry from the niche an admin entered, the type, angle and offer from the email's facts, the season from its words; brand size is `unknown`, and without a niche the email stays untagged. The worker stores sections a batch of 6 at a time (`record_section_tags`, with the tag version, so a retry asks only what is left), then the email's labels and facts once per version (`record_inbox_tags`); an email already tagged finishes without a call, and an email cut short by time or a spent platform budget is put back without using a try (`defer_platform_job`, tag jobs only). Facts version 2: every email tagged by the old one-of-20 labeller is queued once more (`inbox.label:<id>:v2`, 30 minutes after the migration, so the new worker is deployed), and the old `record_inbox_labels` is revoked from the worker. The email labels are on `inbox_messages` (admins) and `competitor_emails` (workspaces that follow the brand); section tags stay admin-only. A model outage fails the job for a retry (`ai_<code>`), at most four tries.
- CI-002 (2026-11-11): **the block library grows from the competitors' sections** (AGENT_MAP §1, D18). A block variant is **structure only** (`features/intelligence/variants.ts`): up to 6 rows of up to 4 columns (each at least a tenth wide), each column a stack of up to 8 slots (image with role, aspect and bleed; heading with size; text with about how many lines; button with style; eyebrow, price, rating, quote, code, divider, social, spacer), with alignment, background (none, brand, accent, dark, image) and padding; never a competitor's words, pictures, links or colours (VISION rule 8). Rows of a single column are one stack (`stackRows`), so a design is the same whether the HTML put each part in its own row or not. Its family, layout, purpose and styles are the section's CI-001 tags (a person's own family label wins). `public.block_variants` holds one row per structure with a **stable name** (`key`: `<family>/seed` for the **20 seed blocks**, the first variant of each family; `<family>/<first 16 hex of the fingerprint>` for one from a competitor; a test compares the migration's seed rows with `seedVariants()`). The duplicate check is the **structural fingerprint** (sha-256 of the family and the stacked shape, widths to tenths, text as short, medium or long): a tagged section's structure is first read from its own HTML (`skeletonOf`, no AI; spacer cells, tracking pixels and the hidden preview line are ignored); when that structure is already a variant, or a section with the same structure already became one, it adds evidence (`evidence` + 1) with no AI call. Only a new structure goes to the **Variant maker** (`variant-maker`, purpose `review`, Flash, with the section's crop, no thinking), once per structure even when an email repeats it; its answer is kept when sure (≥ 0.6) and drawable (columns fill the row, at most 24 slots, the family's own parts), otherwise the HTML structure is kept when drawable, otherwise the section is left out and logged (`unusable`; remembered, so the same structure costs no second call, only when a model really answered or it is far too large; `no_structure` when nothing could be read) (D16). An unreadable, refused or cut-off answer falls back the same way; a timeout, a rate limit or a spent budget keeps what the batch already answered and tries the job again or puts it back. With the fake AI the HTML structure is used and nothing is metered. Only emails assigned to a brand feed the library (an unknown sender's when an admin assigns it). Work runs as `inbox.variant` platform jobs, one per tagged email, queued when its tags are recorded (and once, by the migration, for emails already tagged), stored a batch of 6 at a time (`record_block_variants`, each section once, so a retry adds no evidence). **Live**, the library is filled by the CI-003 tagging run: first, within `variantShareMs` (12 s), with the same 10-second call cap, then tagging for the rest of the run; the sweep places sections only with the sample rules (after tagging; `InboxStep.variants` is null live). **Search** (`search_block_variants` / `searchBlockVariants`): by family, layout, purpose, style and source, and by the emails' labels (industry, email type, offer type, season, angle, brand size; one value or a list of up to 20), most seen first; any active member of an active workspace, platform admins and the agents may search; only structures come back, never a section, an email or a brand, and never by brand. **A member's search by the emails' labels only counts structures seen at 3 brands or more**, so it never describes one brand's email; the agents (service role) and platform admins have no minimum. Results carry `id` (the stable name), `evidenceCount`, `matches` and `score` (null until LRN-002). The table itself and where a variant came from stay with platform admins. A **rate limit** now puts a tag or variant job back without using a try (`waitFor`: the provider's own wait, at least 5 minutes, at most a day), like a spent platform budget (an hour); both stop the run and log a warning. A later re-tag does not place a section again: a new tag version needs a `variants-v2` job key.
- ONB-101 (2026-11-11): **onboarding sources write findings** (AGENT_MAP §2, D19). `public.brand_findings` holds one fact per row about a workspace's brand: source (site, catalog, klaviyo_history, ads, reviews, social), topic from a closed list with one value shape per topic (`features/onboarding/findings.ts`, checked again by `private.finding_value_ok`, never null: `free_shipping` {kind always|never|over, amount, currency}, `returns` and `guarantee` {days}, `delivery_days` {min, max, region?}, `locale` {language, currency}, `price_band` {min, max, currency}, `claim` {subject brand|product, productId?}, `review_platform` {name, verified, rating?, count?}; `shipping`, `offering`, `audience`, `tone`, `contact` in words only; money in major units, to the cent, with its currency), a plain statement, the evidence (for the site: an https page on the brand's own domain or one of its subdomains, read in this run, and the exact words from its visible text, at least a few words, holding the value's numbers), a confidence, the pass (sample rules or a model) and the model. A run replaces the source's current findings from the kinds of page it read (a page not read, or whose answer was unusable, refused or cut off, replaces nothing; an older run that finishes after a newer one replaces nothing). Active members read their own workspace's findings (`listFindings`: current only, newest first, by source, topic and minimum confidence); only the worker writes them. Agreed with lane A for the ONB-106 Brand strategist, which takes the stricter fact and never states a claim it cannot back. **Site reader** (ONB-101): the owner starts it (`start_site_reading`, once a day, on the plain domain without `www.`; asking again after a failed read starts it again). **A site read is never taken by the workspace jobs** (an owner's "Run jobs" or the sweep: `claim_job` now skips `brand.read_site`, like the paid rewrites), only by the **onboarding readers' run** (`GET /api/internal/onboarding/readers`, called by the `Onboarding readers` GitHub Actions schedule at minutes 7, 22, 37 and 52, off until `SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED`, which asks first and stops when nothing waits): it takes reads as the worker (`claim_site_reading`), starts none after 15 seconds, and caps every model call at 10 seconds. A read takes the home page, finds its shipping, returns, help, story and up to two product pages from its own links (at most 8 pages), reads them 3 at a time within 30 seconds (no page is fetched without the time for its fetch and one model call, and no model call starts without its 10 seconds), asks the `site-reader` agent (purpose `review`, Flash, no thinking, on the workspace's own AI budget) for each page's facts, keeps a fact repeated on every page once (from the first page), and stores them all at once (`record_site_findings`). A page that cannot be read is logged and skipped (D16); an AI outage or rate limit fails the job for a retry (earlier findings stay); a spent budget stops it. **The live website is read only with `SITE_READER_MODE=live`** (the owner's switch, like `KLAVIYO_MODE`): https only on 443 (also on every redirect, at most 3, each hop checked again), the brand's own domain and its subdomains only (never a look-alike), every address looked up and refused when private, loopback, link-local or a cloud metadata address, no cookies or credentials, HTML only, at most 8 pages, 1.5 MB and 6 seconds per page and 30 seconds per read; with no model set up, a live site is not read (no fact is guessed from a real page). By default, and always for a synthetic `.example` brand, the sample reader serves a sample site and the honest sample rules read its plain facts at no cost; it never invents a page for a real brand.
- ONB-102 (2026-11-11): **the Klaviyo catalog, read, cleaned and analysed** (AGENT_MAP §2, D19, VISION Step 3 and §10). The owner starts a catalog read (`start_catalog_reading`, once a day; Klaviyo must be connected, and a sample connection is read only for a synthetic brand, checked again when it is stored: a real brand never gets sample products). Like site reads, it is never taken by the workspace jobs (`claim_job` skips `brand.read_catalog`; the sweep dispatches it separately; an owner cannot fail, record or pause a source read, `private.onboarding_lease`), only by the **onboarding readers' run**, which now takes both kinds (`claim_source_reading`, `source_readings_waiting`, `runSourceReadings`; same route, workflow and 59 s timing). **Live Klaviyo is read only with `KLAVIYO_MODE=live`** (the owner's switch, KLV-001), the pinned revision and the stored key (`klaviyo_api_key`), every request capped at 6 s and a rate limit never waited out inside the run. The addresses, fields, includes, filters and page sizes were checked against Klaviyo's published OpenAPI spec for revision 2026-10-15 (github.com/klaviyo/openapi): **the catalog API shows only catalogs sent to it directly (`$custom`), so a Shopify store's products are built from its Viewed Product events and order lines**. A read takes the account (currency, language), the catalog with its variants, the store's Placed Order and Viewed Product metrics (a store platform's own first), then, without a catalog, up to 5 pages of views before the orders (the views are the catalog; orders have no pages), and the orders, all of the last 90 days. Shopify's Viewed Product sends `Name` and its formatted money ("$89.00", "1.299,00 €"); an integration sends `ProductName` and numbers: both are read, and a viewed product missing its title or price takes it from its order lines. Code cleans it (`features/onboarding/catalog/clean.ts`): variant endings stripped ("Weekend Knit - Navy / L" is "Weekend Knit") and variants grouped into one product (lowest price; stock added up only when every variant says it and none is missing from the answer; items listed one per variant merged, with their sales added up); prices to the minor unit of the currency; the orders' currency (`$extra.currency`, `$currency_code`…) checked against the account (`matched`, `mismatch`when 80% or more are in another one: then nothing is featurable and no price band or locale is stated,`unverified`); a photo required (a "no image" placeholder is none); a compare-at price shown only as a temporary sale (seen at full price earlier in the window), never a permanent one; units sold in 30 and 90 days and bestseller ranks; new arrivals only when it can be known (events reaching back 60 days in the stream that first saw the product; a catalog in Klaviyo for 60 days). A record the database would refuse (a repeated id, a price that does not fit, a page with spaces or over 2000 characters, a one-letter title) is left out and counted, never failing the read. **Featured by default** (VISION §10): every clean product of a store of 20 or fewer, or with no sales yet; otherwise the top 20 bestsellers and the new arrivals; a product at no price never; the rest are `not_picked`. A read sets only this default: **an owner's own pick, when a screen adds one, goes in its own column**, never overwritten by a read. The **Catalog analyst** (`catalog-analyst`, purpose `review`, Flash, no thinking, one call, the workspace's budget) names duplicates (code keeps only alike titles at prices within 15%, keeps the one that sold more and moves the sales to it) and writes what the brand sells in a sentence (no numbers, prices or claims); without a model, or when it fails, the catalog is stored without it. Everything is stored at once (`record_catalog_reading`): the products join `public.products`(source`klaviyo`, kept by their Klaviyo id, synthetic when sample; a **whole** read (every page, record and metric read) retires, never deletes, what it no longer sees; the browser roles now only read `products`), `public.catalog_status`(per workspace:`ready`, `empty`= "no products" (D16, logged; only after a whole read: a read that found nothing before its time ran out is tried again instead),`paused`; source `api`or`events`; counts, currency and its check, whether the read was whole) and the catalog findings in `brand_findings` (`price_band`and`locale` counted by code, pass **`code`**, a new pass; `offering`from the analyst, pass`model`with the model's name; evidence`{ref: "klaviyo:..."}`; money in the store's currency, to the cent). A read replaces only the catalog findings it states again (all of them for a store with no products); an older read finishing after a newer one changes nothing. **Klaviyo pausing a read** (`pause_source_reading`): a rate limit puts it back for as long as Klaviyo says (one minute to a day) without using a try, at most 6 times and for at most a day, then the read ends and the owners are told; a rejected key or a missing scope ends a live read (auth), makes the live connection `expired`with the reason (the key is no longer read; connecting again with a checked key fixes it), marks a rejected key revoked, sets the status`paused` and tells the owners in plain words, unless the owner connected a new key after the read began (then the read is only put back); a sample read Klaviyo refused only ends. A key without the Catalogs scope reads the events instead. **KLV-001's deferred guard, on every path:** a live Klaviyo connection changes only through its key check (`connect_klaviyo_live`, which marks its own change), by being disconnected, or when Klaviyo refuses its key: `set_fixture_connection`and`connect_sample_provider`with the same hint are refused (PT409,`private.guard_live_klaviyo`); a sample key over the live one still makes it a sample connection. Lane A reads the catalog with `listCatalog` (`features/onboarding/catalog/read.ts`: current Klaviyo products, best sellers first, money formatted, a compare-at only for a sale, stock null when unknown) and `assemblyProducts`(the assembly's`CatalogProduct`, products with a photo only). **Integration note:** an events-built product's stock is unknown (`stock_known = false`, stored as 0); the older planning, delivery and render commands that require stock above 0 seen in the last day would refuse a campaign pointing at one, and the older product pickers list every product, Klaviyo's retired and hidden ones too: whoever wires them to the catalog reads `stock_known`, `featurable`and`retired_at`.
- ONB-103 (2026-11-11): **the Klaviyo historian** (AGENT_MAP §2, D19). The owner starts a history read (`start_history_reading`, on the same terms as a catalog read: Klaviyo connected, the sample account only for a synthetic brand, once a day); only the onboarding readers' run takes it (`brand.read_history`; the sweep, `claim_job` and owners never do), with every Klaviyo request capped at 6 s, no rate limit waited out, and the 59 s timing. It reads, read-only, with the addresses checked against Klaviyo's published OpenAPI spec for revision 2026-10-15 (campaigns are now campaigns → messages → variations): the account (currency, timezone), the store's Placed Order metric, the email campaign messages sent in the last year and those scheduled (`/api/campaign-messages`, with their campaign and variations: subject, preview, channel), the scheduled campaigns' audiences, the campaign report of the last year and the flow report of the last 90 days (`/api/campaign-values-reports` and `/api/flow-values-reports`: Klaviyo's report questions are a POST, nothing is written; the client gains `query`), the flows, lists and segments. **Flows need `flows:read`, which reading the store does not require**: without it the flows are left out and the read goes on; the key check now lists Flows as a later scope (a warning) and asks for it in the new-key steps. Code counts the history (`features/onboarding/history/facts.ts`): each sent email with its recipients, opens, clicks, orders, revenue and rates; emails a week over the last 8 weeks; the hour most went out at, in the store's timezone; the best emails by revenue per recipient (100 recipients or more); the emails scheduled ahead, on their day in store time, with their lists and segments and whether they speak of an offer; each flow's results; the lists and segments. It is stored as one checked history per workspace (`public.klaviyo_history`: ready, empty when nothing was sent or scheduled, paused when the key needs the owner; members read it; only the worker writes it), replaced by each read (an older read finishing late changes nothing). The **Klaviyo historian** (`klaviyo-historian`, `review`, Flash, no thinking, one call, the workspace's budget) says in words what made money, what the emails promote, how they speak and who they go to; a sentence with numbers, prices or claims, or citing an email the history does not have, is left out. Findings (`brand_findings`, source `klaviyo_history`, evidence `{ref: "klaviyo:campaign:<id>"}`, `klaviyo:campaigns`, `klaviyo:audiences`): the cadence and the best email counted by code (pass `code`), the historian's words (pass `model`), the sample rules' audience line for the sample account. **New findings topic `performance`** (what made money, words only) for the history (and later readers); a read replaces the history findings of the topics it states again. Lane A reads it with `readKlaviyoHistory` and `planFromHistory` (`features/onboarding/history/read.ts`): the fields their `StrategyData` plan input takes (emails a week rounded into 2 to 4, the usual send time within 07:00–21:00, the active lists and segments, the days already scheduled in the 15-day window with their audience and offer, the latest results with title, day, clicks and revenue). Pauses (`pause_source_reading`) work as for catalog reads; a history read's pause is written on the history (kept), not on the catalog status.
- ONB-104 (2026-11-11): **the Ads analyst** (AGENT_MAP §2, D19, D23). The owner starts an ads read (`start_ads_reading`: the brand's website is needed, once a day, a failed read started again; a synthetic brand reads the sample ads, a real brand its live ads); only the onboarding readers' run takes it (`brand.read_ads`; the sweep, `claim_job` and owners never do). The ads come through a typed port, `AdLibrary` (`lib/providers/ad-library.ts`, chosen by the read's mode, never by how its domain looks; a request it no longer waits for is aborted): a source answers with the active ads it finds, or `not_set_up`, `refused`, `unavailable`, `rate_limited`. `ownAds` keeps only what the analyst may read: the brand's own page (`ownPage`: of the pages whose live ads send people to the brand's own domain or a subdomain, one named like the brand first, then the one with the most such ads, each ad counted once; two equally likely pages are never guessed between; a reseller's or an affiliate's page, or a look-alike domain, is left out), active ads, each once, newest first, at most 30, at most 4 texts of each kind of 600 characters with no control, zero-width or direction characters, a link only on the brand's own domain and kept as a browser reads it (the database checks the same address, `private.ad_link_ok`). **The sample library serves sample ads for a synthetic brand (`*.example`) only; a real brand's live Ad Library is not read yet** (the route is the owner's choice: Meta's official Ad Library API needs a developer token and shows ordinary product ads only when they ran in the EU and UK; reading the public website automatically goes against Meta's terms; a paid service needs its own key; asked through the supervisor 11 Nov), so a real brand's read is logged and skipped. Code counts the landing pages the ads send people to (the three most used, without their tracking) and the formats and places they run in (pass `code`); the **Ads analyst** (`ads-analyst`, `review`, Flash, no thinking, one call, the workspace's budget) says what they offer (`promotion`, `free_shipping`), why they say to buy (`angle`), what they show (`creative`), what they sell, who they speak to and how; a finding citing an ad not read, or quoting words that are not in one of the ad's texts, is left out (the quote is kept as the ad writes it); an offer is kept only in an offer's own words next to its number ("20% off" but never "30% recycled" or "up to 50% off", "$10 off" with a currency its sign may stand for, "free gift", "bundle" or "buy two, get one", free shipping "over" an amount, never always free when the ad names a threshold), and its statement is written from its value (never the model's numbers); words-only topics with numbers or claims (organic, handmade, sustainable, proven…) are left out; the sample rules read the plain offers of the sample ads. Each finding's evidence is the ad's own Ad Library page (`https://www.facebook.com/ads/library/?id=<id>`, an ad kept in this read, checked again by `record_ad_findings`) and the ad's exact words. **New findings topics** for ads: `promotion` (`percent_off` with a whole percent 1–90, `amount_off` with an amount and currency, `gift`, `bundle`), `angle` and `creative` (words only) and `landing_page` (`{url}`, https on the brand's own domain); the shapes in `topicValues` are strict (an extra key is refused, as the database does). A read that found the brand's ads, or whose library has no live ads for it, replaces the earlier ads findings (an ended offer is no longer current); a skipped read (no library, a refusal, ads of no page that is the brand's own) replaces nothing, but a real brand's read never leaves sample findings standing; an older read finishing late replaces nothing (its findings are kept already replaced). An outage or a rate limit of the library (or a library that fails instead of answering), or of the AI, fails the job for another try (earlier findings stay); on the job's last try an unreadable library is logged and skipped instead, and an AI outage leaves the ads without words, as does an AI failure no try will mend (no model set up, the account refused) on any try (what code counted is stored). An unusable answer or a spent budget leaves the ads without words (the counted findings stand). The sweep never takes an ads read (`separatelyDispatchedKinds`).
- INB-003 (2026-11-07): an inbox email is cut into **sections from its HTML structure**, never by the model. The sections are the rows of the 500–760px content column (or of all the stacked 600px boxes MJML-style builders make); single-cell wrappers are looked through, a line of words with links or bold parts in it and columns standing side by side stay one block, spacers, the hidden preview line and tracking pixels are dropped, prose without a layout table stays one block, and there are at most 40. Hidden means `display:none`, `visibility:hidden`, `opacity:0`, the `hidden` attribute, or `max-height:0` with `overflow:hidden`; `font-size:0` hides only the words directly in it. Each section is `text` or `design` (design = a picture and fewer than 30 words). The image job keeps the images first, then stores the sections (`email_sections`, once per email) and a marked copy of the HTML: the original bytes with only `data-gr-section="n"` written into each row's start tag, so the doctype and entities stay as sent. When the sections cannot be stored the images are still recorded and the email simply has no sections. The screenshot browser crops one picture per section (`sect/<message>/<n>.jpg`, within 70 s of the two-minute lease) and reports where each sits. `record_inbox_sections` queues one `inbox.label` job an hour out; `record_inbox_screenshot` now takes `boxes` (the four-argument version is dropped) and brings that job forward. An email without HTML is queued for labelling at once (facts from its subject). Emails INB-002 handled before this migration are cut again (`inbox.assets:<id>:sections`, images not fetched again) and photographed again for their crops. The label job picks one of the 20 block types (`features/blocks/catalog.ts`) per section through the `SectionLabeller` port: today a rule-based sample labeller that says `unsure` rather than guess. `blockLabeller(runFor)` puts Lane A's `labelSection` (BLK-003, `features/blocks/labeller.ts`) behind the same port, one metered call per stored section: once GEM-001..003 are merged the sweep passes `(id) => (request) => runAi({ tenantId: null, callerKey: \`section:${id}\`, purpose: 'label', request }, { ledger: new SupabaseBudgetLedger(createServiceClient()) })`, which GEM-003 meters against the platform's daily cap (competitor sections belong to no workspace); until then it uses the sample labeller. Its answer is checked before it is stored (an unknown type is `unsure`, the confidence 0..1, the source `sample`or`gemini`; the database refuses `owner`from the worker). It works out the email's **micro facts** without AI: subject, preview line, the strongest offer (percent, money off, BOGO, gift, free shipping; the top bar, the logo and menu and the footer are not the email's offer), the code (capitals after "code" or "use"), angle (fixed rules: restock, seasonal, launch, sale, best seller, reviews, story, how-to, bundle), the main call to action (outside the bar, menu and footer), send time (UTC; shown in the reader's brand timezone), block sequence and text share.`record_inbox_labels`stores them **once per extraction version** (a repeat says`already`) on the message and its competitor email; a label the owner gave is never overwritten. The **brand summary** (macro facts) is computed when read, from the brand's labelled emails of the last 90 days: emails per week (since its first email, at least a week), angle mix, offer share and usual percent, text:design, usual send day and hour (once two emails share it), and patterns (angle + offer + opening blocks) where a repeated pattern (2 or more) is weighted above a one-off. Sections are for platform admins; workspaces read the facts of brands they follow. Inspiration shows "What they send" per followed brand, with a Sample marker on sample brands.
- INB-004 (2026-11-07): what the inbox cannot handle goes to **one admin page, `/admin/inbox`** ("Inspiration inbox" in the admin menu): the ops queue ("Needs a person") and the brand directory. Every task kind has plain words and Mark done / Dismiss (`resolve_ops_task`, admins only, PT409 when already closed or gone). An unknown sender, or a sender not registered for its `+alias`, also gets "Assign to this brand" (`admin_assign_sender`: sample senders (`.example`) go to sample brands and real senders to real ones (23514 otherwise), the sender is remembered for the brand, every waiting email from it or a subdomain is assigned, and its tasks close; PT409 when another brand owns the sender); one email can be assigned on its own (`admin_assign_inbox_message`; its sender's task closes once no other email from that sender waits, otherwise it points at the next one). The directory lists each brand with the **address to subscribe with** (the inbox's own address with the brand's `+alias`: `capture+<alias>@inbox.example` for sample brands, the Gmail address for real ones, or a note to set `INBOX_IMAP_USER`), niche, subscription status, last email, newsletter count and sender domains (`admin_inbox_brand_counts`, counted in the database), and adds or edits a brand. **Subscription status tells the truth:** a brand is `subscribed` only once an ordinary newsletter arrives; a double-opt-in confirmation sets it back to `pending` (unless its newsletters already arrive, then the confirmation is ignored) and a click proves nothing; "you have been unsubscribed" sets `unsubscribed` and opens a resubscribe task; marking that task done sets `pending` again. `competitor_captures.pending_since` says since when a brand waits; each sweep's `check_pending_subscriptions` opens one `inbox_pending_subscription` task after a week without a newsletter, never again for the same wait once it is closed. **Signals** (`features/inbox/signals.ts`, fixed English rules, strict): "confirm your subscription", "verify your email" and the like are a confirmation (a discount in it is fine); "you have been unsubscribed" is a notice, but not with win-back or sunset words ("we miss you", "you will be removed unless…", "% off"). The poll worker notes every kept email, also when a retry finds it already recorded (`flag_inbox_message`: the text part of an email waiting for a brand, and its signal, acted on once). Confirmations and notices are not newsletters: they leave the Inspiration library and are never counted. **Clicking:** a known brand's confirmation with an https link queues an `inbox.confirm` job; the worker opens the link once like a person (a plain GET, https on port 443, no cookies, the image fetcher's private-address checks) **only** while the link and every redirect stay on the brand's own domain, one of its sender domains or the confirm and tracking domains of a well-known email service (Klaviyo, Mailchimp's list-manage, Omnisend, Shopify Email, MailerLite, ConvertKit, Brevo, Attentive, Postscript; never hosts where anyone can publish a page, such as ck.page or mailchi.mp); anything else is refused and left to a person. `begin_confirmation_click` logs `attempted` before the link is opened, so a retry never opens it twice; `record_confirmation_click` logs the outcome and where the link ended, once (`inbox_confirmation_clicks`, admins read it); a refused or failed click opens an `inbox_opt_in` task. Sample brands are "clicked" without the network. **Hand labels** (for Lane A's Step 1 gate): `email_sections` keeps the machine label (`block_type`, `label_source` sample or gemini, `confidence`, `label_model`) apart from a person's own (`owner_block_type`, `owner_labelled_by`, `owner_labelled_at`, written only by `admin_label_section`); the facts' block sequence uses the person's label when there is one. Owners, staff and anon are refused by the database (42501) and by the page (the admin surface).
- KLV-001 (2026-11-07): **live Klaviyo is the owner's switch, `KLAVIYO_MODE=live`**, set in Vercel; anything else, or nothing, keeps the sample connection (the format check only, nothing sent to Klaviyo), also on a hosted database; any value is accepted, so a typo never breaks other pages. Local runs and the browser journeys force `KLAVIYO_MODE=fake` (`scripts/local-runtime.mjs`). While live, the Sample data tools neither show nor accept a Klaviyo fixture status, except disconnecting. In live mode the Connections screen checks the pasted private key with Klaviyo before anything is saved: one small read-only GET per scope (`lib/providers/klaviyo/check-key.ts`), because no Klaviyo endpoint lists a key's scopes. Needed now (refused when any is missing, with Klaviyo's own words for making a new key): Accounts, Catalogs, Campaigns, Events, Lists, Metrics and Segments, Read Access. Needed later for drafts (a warning only): Templates and Images. Write scopes cannot be proven by a read; they are checked when first used. The account's id, name, website (as an https origin), timezone, currency and country fill the brand facts. **The client** (`lib/providers/klaviyo/client.ts`, server-only) talks only to `https://a.klaviyo.com` with the pinned revision **`2026-10-15`** (`KLAVIYO_API_REVISION` defaults to it; a different value stops live checks instead of mixing contracts), JSON:API headers, no redirects, no cache, an 8 MB cap, typed failures (`bad_key`, `missing_scope`, `rate_limited`, `unavailable`, `rejected`, `invalid_response`), a short rate limit waited out at most twice, `links.next` followed only on Klaviyo's host, and the key never in an error or a log. Tests use recorded answers (`recorded.ts`), never the network. **The key is kept in Supabase Vault**, not with `CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`: `klaviyo_credentials` holds which Vault secret it is, the masked hint, the Klaviyo account id, the revision, what later steps lack and its status; members read everything but the secret's id, nobody writes the table directly. `connect_klaviyo_live` (owners only; every check before any write, so no error can echo the key; a stale secret under the workspace's name is replaced) saves the connection with `workspace_connections.mode = 'live'` (allowed for Klaviyo only). `klaviyo_api_key(t)` gives the key back to the server only (`require_service`), for an active workspace whose live connection is connected. Disconnecting, or a sample connection with another key replacing a live one, deletes the key and its secret and makes the connection a sample one. `begin_klaviyo_key_check` allows 5 checks per workspace in 15 minutes (PT409 after).

The sidebar is 255px and collapses to a 56px icon rail. Collapse and group state live in the `gr_shell` cookie (`components/shell/shell-state.ts`; the server reads it, so the first paint is right). `ShellNavigation` is now `{ primary, groups }`, and groups can hold SOON items that are never links. Agents › Bill Optimizer links to the existing fixture `/optimizer` page (the other four agents stay SOON). The M1.5 1:1 rebuild overrides UX-024 (no dead agent menus) for now. `Button` gained an `outline` variant and a `shape` variant (`control` 12px default | `pill`); the radius moved from the base class into `shape`. `AppShell` is async (it reads the cookie), and `ShellFrame` takes `demo: { label, description } | null` from the brand presentation. `ShellIdentity` has `email`, read from the session only when its `sub` matches the request's user. `/campaigns/:id` (today's local campaign review) highlights Campaigns; the §4 Klaviyo campaign detail page will need its own path.

- UIR-002 part 3 (2026-11-06): the shared components from spec §2.
  - `lib/campaign-status.ts` is the one CAMPAIGN status vocabulary (Draft, With designer, Needs your approval, Scheduled, Sent, Cancelled, Missed; UIR-015 wires it to data).
  - `lib/status-vocabulary.ts` stays for non-campaign states. Shared words use the same meaning, and `PillTone` extends `StatusTone`.
  - `AlertDialog` is the yes/no confirm. `ConfirmDialog` stays the wrapper for a form that needs a confirming step.
  - `Modal` asks before discarding typed answers, even when the browser forces it shut, and ignores events from nested dialogs.

- UIR-002 part 4 (2026-11-06): owner-facing dates and times come from `lib/brand-time.ts`.
  - Formats: `formatBrandDateTime` gives `Oct 7, 2026 · 10:49 PM`; `formatBrandDate`; `timezoneLabel` gives "Sydney time", and "UTC-5" for `Etc/` zones; `relativeTime` gives "4 weeks ago", with months only from 5 weeks and never "12 months".

  - `RefreshButton` is the pending-aware outline Refresh for a form with a server action.

- UIR-011 (2026-11-06): `/settings` follows `docs/UI_REBUILD_SPEC.md` §11 and OWNER_JOURNEYS §11. New `campaign_defaults` (one row per workspace: featured product (FK to the workspace's own product, cleared if the product is removed), sender name, https primary link, send-to and don't-send-to Klaviyo list/segment ids (at most 20 each, never in both), include footer, footer source `auto` only). Members read via RLS; only the owner saves, through `save_campaign_defaults` (workspace lock first, `PT409` on a stale page). All five tabs live in one form and one "Save defaults" saves them. The Timezone card shows the workspace timezone, the same one the Dashboard uses (OWNER_JOURNEYS fix), and says it is not read from Klaviyo yet; the clock ticks every 15 s. Audience options are the sample Klaviyo lists and segments while Klaviyo is a fixture connection. Footer templates appear once real Klaviyo exists. Defaults are saved now but no draft reads them yet: the page says so, and the browser lane's new-draft flow (UIR-017) is the first consumer (`campaign_defaults` row; types in `lib/supabase/database.types.ts`). The page opens on Featured Product (no remembered tab). The old operations panels (budget, notifications, holidays until `/holidays`, delivery policy) moved unchanged under "More settings". Migrations numbered `20260914090020` and `20260914090021` (review fixes: Klaviyo-style audience ids in a one-dimensional list, sender name without control characters and trimmed, https link to a real host, `updated_by` cleared when a login is deleted) so they apply after 90019 on the hosted project; merge and apply in number order. The form uses `noValidate`: our checks run on save and a refused field opens its tab, gets `aria-invalid` and focus. Saved audiences and the saved product are kept when Klaviyo or the catalogue cannot load; the saved product is always offered even past the first 200. All copy is in the future tense because no draft reads the defaults yet. A timezone the runtime does not know is shown as UTC (`validTimezone`). Load failures are told apart: missing table, load error, demo.

- UIR-012 (2026-11-06): `/holidays` follows `docs/UI_REBUILD_SPEC.md` §12 and OWNER_JOURNEYS §12. `holidays` gains `kind` ('holiday' | 'event', existing rows become events), `repeats_annually`, `revision` and `updated_at`; members read via RLS, direct writes are revoked, and only the owner writes through `save_holiday` (add with `holiday_id` null and revision 0, or edit; `PT409` when changed or removed elsewhere), `delete_holiday` and `set_holiday_country` (new `holiday_preferences(tenant_id, country)`, one of US, GB, AU, CA, NZ, IN, INTL). `private.planning_holidays` now returns a yearly entry on its next date within the 30-day window (29 February becomes 28 February) and adds `kind` to each object (the planner's zod schema ignores extra keys). Platform holidays are computed, never stored: `features/holidays/platform.ts` `platformHolidays({ country, from, days })` returns `{ date, name, category }` (civic, cultural, commercial) for the browser lane's planner chips; `countryFromTimezone` gives the first guess. `features/holidays/entries.ts` holds `nextOccurrence` and `ownEntries` for the planner's "YOURS (N)". The sidebar's Holidays & Events links to `/holidays`; the old holiday panel is removed from Settings. Migration numbered `20260914090022` so it applies after 90021 on the hosted project. Review fixes in `20260914090023`: `private.next_occurrence` never returns a date before the saved date (a yearly entry that starts next year is not planned this year); dates must be 1900–2999 and finite (also a `not valid` check on the table, so old rows are untouched); the old `add_holiday(t, name, event_date, notes)` now calls `save_holiday` (one-off event, same checks, logged); `set_holiday_country` writes `holiday_country_set` to `workspace_events`; `holidays` grants are select-only for `authenticated` and nothing for `anon` or `service_role`.
