  - `features/klaviyo-data/reader.ts` decides what owner screens may show:
    - `sample` only while the workspace's Klaviyo connection (`workspace_connections`) is `connected` (or without a database in demo sign-in);
    - `not_connected` with the reason otherwise;
    - `unavailable` in real mode until a live adapter exists. The live adapter must read a tenant-scoped snapshot written by the sync job, not call Klaviyo during the request.

- UIR-004 (2026-11-06): Klaviyo detail pages.

An earlier version matched every signed-in page; the browser journeys on the server-action-heavy pages (campaign workflow, references) then failed twice, so those pages stay out of the proxy until that is proven safe. This is a foundation-config change, recorded here.

- UIR-015 (2026-11-06): every campaign shows ONE status, derived by `features/campaigns/status.ts` from the real state machine and never picked by hand. The order of checks:
  1. Sent, Cancelled and Scheduled come only from delivery, and only once the provider confirmed them.
  2. Failed, blocked and unconfirmed (`reconciling`) deliveries are a **Delivery problem**, a new status (danger), whatever the clock says.
  3. An in-flight delivery (queued … scheduling) is Scheduled for a future time ("Next: Waiting for confirmation"), and a Delivery problem once its time passed: the worker refuses past times, and the date can't be edited while delivery is active.
  4. Anything else whose time passed is Missed ("Next: Pick a new date").
  5. Needs your approval: a new or moved date first; then With designer (assigned, or revisions on a design email); then copy or a delivered design waiting; then the final approve-and-schedule step, once the date, copy AND design are approved. Text emails get a text design the owner reviews, so they pass the design step too.
  6. Otherwise Draft, including a turned-down date (it can be revised and approved again; copy and design approvals wait for it).

  Missed and Delivery problem campaigns have their own "Needs a look" tile and filter, and come first in the dashboard's "Waiting for you".

  Replaced plan entries show "Replaced". The overview's "Next:", its tiles and filters, the dashboard's "Waiting for you" and upcoming sends, and the calendar's month line ("4 campaigns · 1 needs your approval · 1 scheduled") all come from the same status; the old "awaiting review · approved" line is gone. The campaign page shows the pill and plain words per step (`stepStateWords`). `hasDeliveryProblem` is removed (the status says it); the pill lives in `features/campaigns/status-pill.tsx`.
  - Still raw: the delivery panel's "Campaign delivery: …" line (e2e asserts it), the planning panel, `/copy-review` and admin designers. GROS replies use their own delivery words (`20260914090016_gros_readable_reply.sql`); integration note for the GROS domain.
  - Render-path revisions on a design email read "With designer", and a text email given to a human designer reads "Draft" once changes are asked, until active designer assignments are loaded with campaigns.

- UIR-006 part 1 (2026-11-06): the Campaigns page is one planner card (`features/planner/`).
  - One list of entries feeds the calendar AND the table: this workspace's campaigns plus the Klaviyo sends (sample data in fake mode). Klaviyo drafts with no send time sit in a "Not scheduled in Klaviyo" row (§15 bug 3), which follows the filters.
  - Every chip shows its status in words on a second line that wraps, plus "Klaviyo sample" or "Synthetic demo", because pill colours repeat between statuses. Klaviyo chips also have a dashed outline in the text colour and a download icon. The legend lists every status, Cancelled and Delivery problem included. The month line counts the whole month, whatever the filters, with the same status ("4 campaigns (1 Klaviyo sample) · 1 needs your approval").
  - Filters (status, channel), the view, the month and "show replaced entries" all live in the URL (`plannerHref`), so changing one keeps the others. The old `?view=list` and `?view=history` links open the table (history also turns on replaced entries). The replaced-entries switch is in both views.
  - Klaviyo chips open `/campaigns/klaviyo/:id?from=planner&<planner state>`. On that page `plannerBack` turns it into "Back to Campaigns" to the exact view; `KlaviyoDetail` and `NotConnected` take an optional `back`.
  - Weeks start on Sunday (spec §6). Every day and time uses the brand timezone, named in the legend and table header.
  - One "+ New" menu (§18.10): "Single send" jumps to the campaign form. "Fill the month (sample plan)" (owners only) jumps to the planning panel (`#planning`); it becomes "Fill the month with AI" when the AI planner lands, so the label never promises AI that isn't used.
  - "+ plan send" (owners, today and later; below the chips so it never covers one) opens `/first-draft?date=YYYY-MM-DD&from=planner&<planner state>`. The form starts at 10:00 that day, or the next hour today, or 10:00 the next calendar day late at night (`draftDefaultDate`), and "Back to Campaigns" returns to the same view.
  - Holiday chips show this workspace's own holidays and events in one tone (no holiday type in the schema yet). `Holidays & Events` links to `/settings#holidays` until the §6.4 modal.
  - `features/campaigns/calendar.tsx` and `buildCampaignCalendar` are removed; `calendar-model.ts` keeps the month and timezone helpers. `pillToneClasses` is exported from `components/ui/pill.tsx` so chips match pill colours.
  - Not built yet: Report export, §6.4–§6.7 popups, inspiration cards (§18.11 makes them a library link).

- DS-006 (2026-11-06): the composer (`features/composer/`) saves a paste through the same `savePastedReference` helper as the paste form (extracted from `importPastedReference` into server-only `features/references/pasted-save.ts`, messages unchanged) and redirects to `/create-email/{id}?mode=copy|rewrite`. The choose page only marks that outcome ("Your choice in the composer", primary button); it never starts a copy or rewrite by itself, so owner confirmation and paid-AI gates are unchanged. EXACT→copy, REBRAND→rewrite, ADAPT disabled with a reason. Subject comes from the HTML `<title>` or first readable line; a link-only paste is refused with an honest message. Composer is owner-only on the dashboard and the setup reference step; the existing Paste/Upload links stay.

- STAB-001 (2026-11-06): `GET /api/internal/jobs` is the scheduled worker. It requires `Authorization: Bearer CRON_SECRET` (existing env contract, unchanged; checked before anything else), runs only when `ADAPTER_MODE` is `fake` or unset, and visits workspaces through the existing `runFixtureJobs` path in bounded batches (≤100 workspaces, ≤4 jobs per visit, ≤3 visits, no new visit after 40 s, `maxDuration` 60). Workspaces with due or stale-leased jobs go first (the active-workspace filter is part of that query); the other active workspaces get daily housekeeping in random order. `email.rewrite` and `email.import` are never dispatched by the scheduler, so it cannot start or retry paid AI. Service-role reads of `tenants` and `job_runs` select IDs only; every claim still goes through `claim_job`/`worker_access`. The former `GET` 405 response is replaced by this handler; `POST` loopback dispatch is unchanged. Triggers: Vercel cron daily (Hobby limit) plus a GitHub Actions schedule every 10 minutes with the repository secret `CRON_SECRET`, fixed to the production HTTPS address and off until the repository variable `SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED` is `true` (skipped runs use no Actions minutes; about 4,400 minutes a month when on). Shared config: `playwright.config.ts` gains a `scheduled-sweep` project that depends on the desktop and mobile projects, because the sweep drains every workspace's queue. See `docs/SCHEDULED_JOBS_RUNBOOK.md`.

- DS-005 (2026-11-06): new shared `EmptyState` (serif line, plain sentence, at most one action). `WorkspaceError` (every role's `error.tsx`) and the root `app/error.tsx` show the opaque `error.digest` as a support reference and say saved work is kept and "Try again" never repeats a save; never put data into a custom digest. Empty-state copy must be role-aware: only owners are told a decision is theirs.

- DS-004 (2026-11-06): `lib/status-vocabulary.ts` is the single list of operator statuses (UX-012: Collecting, Ready, Needs setup, In production, Needs review, Scheduled, Sending, Sent, Failed) with label, tone and meaning. `StatusMark` gains `status` (vocabulary) and `info`/`success` tones; `active` stays as an alias. New `StatGrid`/`StatTile` (unknown values render “—” with a reason, never 0) and `SplitSection` (inverse panel uses the `--gr-inverse*` tokens directly; not tenant-themable yet). `Panel` gains an optional `eyebrow`.

- DS-003 (2026-11-06): `BrandPresentation.theme` gains `onSecondary` (text on the blue key action, published as `--on-brand-secondary`; checked by the contrast gate on `secondary` and its hover `ink`). `Button` gains `variant="accent"` for the one key action per screen; `secondary` is now a light grey fill without an outline. `controlClass` moved to `components/ui/control.ts` (still re-exported from `components/ui/workspace.tsx`) and `TextField` uses it, so every input shares one style. New shared components: `SegmentedControl` (native radio group) and `TextLink` (arrow link). Native checkboxes/radios use the ink colour.

- DS-001 (2026-11-06): the default white-label theme (`defaultBrandPresentation`) is now built from `lib/design/tokens.ts`, and `app/globals.css` maps the existing semantic variables (`--surface`, `--text`, `--line`, `--brand-*`, `--danger*`, `--warning*`, `--radius-*`) to the `--gr-*` tokens. Variable names are unchanged, so no component contract changed. `textFaint` uses `ink-muted` (AA on the canvas). Added tokens `ink-strong` and `danger-strong` for hover states. Tailwind 4 `@theme` gains design names (`bg-canvas`, `text-ink`, `border-hairline`, `font-serif`, `font-mono`, `tracking-display`) that resolve through the white-label variables; every `rounded-*` step is 4px. Fonts: Newsreader, Geist, Geist Mono via `next/font/google` (Inter removed). The `data-design-seed` attribute was removed. Dark mode stays off until a dark `BrandPresentation` variant exists.

- DOC-003 (2026-11-06): planning moves to seven milestones M0–M6 in `docs/ROADMAP.md`, with GitHub issues as the work queue (one per P0 register item plus `STAB-*`/`DS-*` project work items; one epic per register category with P1/P2 checklists). `.github/project/roadmap.json` is the source for labels, milestones and the milestone of every issue; `scripts/github/project-plan.mjs` derives issue text from it and `docs/feature-register.json`. The GitHub connector used by agents cannot create labels or milestones, so `.github/workflows/project-sync.yml` applies them with `GITHUB_TOKEN` once GitHub Actions is enabled; until then labels are GitHub's default grey and issues have no milestone. New visual language: `docs/DESIGN.md` with tokens in `lib/design/tokens.ts` and `app/styles/tokens.css` (`--gr-*`), not imported by the app until DS-001. The owner palette is kept exactly; added `hairline-strong`, `accent-text`, `on-accent`, `on-ink`, inverse and muted semantic tokens so every allowed pair meets WCAG AA (dark-mode accent button uses black text; `ink-subtle` small text only on white). The old root `DESIGN.md` describes the live theme until M1 completes; `PRODUCT.md` brand commitments now record the owner's new default visual direction. White-label stays data-driven: a customer theme may replace colours and fonts only if every `contrastRules` pair passes. Issue IDs are ledger task IDs; `ONB-001` and `REP-001` issues continue their existing rows. An extra `epic` label marks category epics.

- REF-003: implemented by Claude Code on the user's sequential request; root delegated these shared integration changes. (1) `postal-mime@4.1.4` pinned exact (zero runtime dependencies, explicit nesting/header/part/forward limits). Mailparser's own documentation marks it maintenance-only and recommends PostalMime; the only published PostalMime advisory (GHSA-f2hq-32vh-53cc) is fixed from 4.0.8. Declared charsets the runtime cannot decode are refused rather than read as windows-1252; `.html`/`.txt` must be UTF-8, BOM-marked or (HTML) `<meta charset>`-declared. (2) Durable pre-upload admission: `admit_email_file_upload` binds actor + tenant + request key + SHA-256 + kind + size + name and reserves capacity under the tenant lock (20/hour, 200 active, 500 added emails, 100 MiB stored-or-reserved distinct bytes) before any service-role write; `finalize_email_file_upload` enqueues one `email.import` job only after the stored object size is confirmed; replays resume at the interrupted step; nothing deletes objects, so an object used by another receipt is never removed. Storage allows overlapping writers to the same path (last wins) but refuses later ones; the path is the SHA-256 of the server-validated bytes and the worker re-verifies, so racing writers store identical bytes. (3) Trusted worker RPCs `claim_/finish_/fail_email_file_import` are service-role only, re-check active tenant and active uploader, and accept parsed output only for the frozen hash and still-stored object. Legacy `claim_job` (selection and stale sweep) and `private.onboarding_lease` (so `fail_job` and fixture finishers) now refuse `email.import` as well as `email.rewrite`; `job_runs_kind_check` gains `email.import`. (4) Trusted format: `reference_imports` gains `source_kind`, `file_import_id`, `content_format`; `create_email_result` is redefined in 90015 to use `content_format` when present (pastes keep the 90014 regex inference), and choose/preview/copy text version/rewrite worker read the same stored format (`sourceFormat`, `email_sources.format`). `pastedReadable` takes an optional trusted format. (5) New private bucket `email-imports` (5 MiB, html/plain/rfc822), no client write policy, owner/admin read policy for referenced objects. `GET /api/v1/email-imports/:id/file` and export `part=file` serve exact bytes; `part=original` keeps its meaning (decoded source). `next.config.ts` applies the sandbox CSP override to both download paths. (6) Server-only pilot gate `EMAIL_IMPORT_TENANT_IDS` (exact UUIDs, max 50, no wildcard) for any non-loopback or production Storage; checked before admission, before the write, on retry and on each worker attempt; independent of the unchanged OpenAI pilot gate. Root must add the blank name to `.env.example` (this session cannot read it). (7) `SubmitButton` gains optional `pendingLabel`/`disabled`; the setup reference step adds a secondary "Upload a file" link beside "Paste an email". (8) Review fixes: metadata/name/download shortening is surrogate-safe (`clipText`, `wellFormed`); HTML decoding follows BOM → explicit `<meta charset>` → UTF-8 and refuses undecodable declarations; dates outside years 1–9999 are dropped as metadata. (9) Narrow shared UI integration requested by root now that real rewriting is being configured: `AppShell` takes an optional `status` (default "Your workspace" / "Emails are saved privately"; admin and designer layouts pass their own truthful line) and shows the brand's demo copy only when `AUTH_MODE=demo`; the mobile menu shows the same status; the client overview footer reads "Sending and performance data use samples. Emails you add and create are saved." outside demo mode. `BrandPresentation` is unchanged, and no simulated sending/results behaviour changed. (10) Final deployment-compatibility correction: Vercel Functions cap request and response bodies at 4.5 MB (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/limitations, https://vercel.com/docs/errors/function_payload_too_large), so the raw upload/storage bound is 4 MiB (4,194,304 bytes) everywhere — browser hint and help text, action, parser, SQL `byte_size` and admission checks, bucket `file_size_limit` — keeping uploads plus multipart overhead and original-file downloads under the cap. Earlier REF-003 evidence used 5 MiB; it was superseded by this change and re-verified. The 100 MiB workspace cap, admission, pilot gate, 100,000-character decoded bound and the existing `serverActions.bodySizeLimit` (used by other upload features) are unchanged; files above 4 MiB need direct-to-Storage signed uploads, recorded as remaining. (11) Root supervisor integration, not overwritten by this task: blank `EMAIL_IMPORT_TENANT_IDS=` in `.env.example`; CI browser job runs `node scripts/test-email-import-concurrency.mjs` with a 25-minute browser timeout; the deployment snapshot was moved outside the repository. A narrow, supervisor-authorized test fix in `tests/e2e/email-generation.spec.ts` makes the preview-fit helper wait (retried poll, no sleep) until the published `data-scale` matches the measured container before the unchanged fit assertions.

- AI-002/REP-001: implemented by Claude Code on the user's explicit request for real OpenAI rewriting and exact source-email replication. This narrowly supersedes the historical "fake AI / structural reuse only" defaults for the pasted-email flow only: (1) a server-only OpenAI Responses adapter (native `fetch`, no SDK) behind the typed `AiProvider` port; `lib/providers/contracts.ts` gains optional `schema`/`reasoningEffort`/`timeoutMs`/`safetyIdentifier` request fields, optional response identity/cache fields and a typed `AiProviderError` (kind + billing `none|usage|unknown`). Default model `gpt-6.1-terra`, priced from the official pricing page; only priced models are accepted. (2) Exact copies: `email_sources` is a separate immutable, byte-exact copy of the owner's paste (update-blocking trigger); copy and rewrite results are immutable versions in `email_result_revisions`, synced as unapproved `campaign_content` revisions, so existing owner approval/design/delivery gates are unchanged and edits invalidate approval as before. (3) Paid work: `claim_/begin_/finish_/fail_email_generation` execute only as `service_role` (server worker), never for signed-in users; they re-check active tenant, active owner requester and frozen brand/source/target revision. Reservations bound the full serialized request (UTF-8 bytes + 2,000 tokens) plus full output; actual cost above a reservation is still recorded. Unknown outcomes are charged at the reservation and never retried automatically. (4) Shared legacy helpers are narrowed for the new kind only: `claim_job` (selection and expired-lease sweep), `private.onboarding_lease` (so `fail_job`/fixture completions refuse it), `reserve_/reconcile_fixture_usage` (refuse the `email-rewrite:` namespace and rewrite reservations); `private.reserve_budget` keeps identical accounting with truthful messages for rewrite spend. `job_runs` gains kind `email.rewrite`; `ai_calls.provider` allows `openai`/`sample`. (5) `parse5@8.0.1` is pinned as a direct dependency (already in the lockfile via jsdom) because sanitizing and editing untrusted email HTML needs a WHATWG parser rather than regex rewriting. (6) The import page now continues to `/create-email/<id>` (rewrite or copy); the outline composer remains one link away. (7) `next.config.ts` adds a later header rule giving `/api/v1/emails/:id/export` a `sandbox; default-src 'none'` CSP, because the app-wide CSP otherwise overrides route headers. (8) Browser tests start a loopback OpenAI test double (`scripts/fake-openai.mjs`); the app accepts plain-HTTP OpenAI base URLs only on loopback outside production. (9) Temporary production pilot gate: because every new workspace receives the synthetic starter package and workspace creation is uncapped, real OpenAI requests are default-deny and allowed only for exact workspace UUIDs in the server-only `EMAIL_REWRITE_TENANT_IDS` (max 50, no wildcard). It is checked on the choose/retry screens and on every worker attempt before reservation or dispatch; not-enabled queued jobs fail as `setup_required` (diagnostic `workspace_not_enabled`). Only the loopback test double outside production bypasses it. This is not a commercial entitlement or billing system; no SQL change.

- REF-002: implemented by Claude Code on the user's sequential request. Owner-pasted reference emails are a separate tenant-private source class (`origin = owner_import`), never competitor evidence or a synthetic watched brand. The original paste is stored exactly as received (CRLF included, up to 100,000 characters) and is immutable to clients; only a server-derived plain-text preview (line endings normalized, bounded at 20,000 UTF-16 code units with an explicit "preview limited" notice; if an astral character straddles the cutoff, only its high half is replaced by a one-unit ellipsis so no lone surrogate reaches JSON/Postgres) is ever displayed, always as React text, never as markup, iframe content or links. Source URLs are http/https without credentials, kept as a note and never fetched. The reusable outline is derived in SQL from paragraph breaks; caller-supplied structure or HTML is rejected. One guarded owner RPC with actor/tenant/payload-bound receipts makes retries idempotent; 500 imports per workspace. `create_first_draft` gains an `imported_reference` source that requires the exact tenant and records provenance (`analysis: none`, copy not reusable) without approval changes. Recent-choice lists are bounded: 12 in the composer (plus a preselected item), 5 on the import page, the latest 1 in setup. Rendering keeps its existing structure choices; an imported outline is not a rendering source. The integration owner narrowly assigned two existing browser specs to this task for test-only synchronization fixes (statistics worker-batch drain; planning approval completion predicate).

- DRF-001: continue sequentially from the user’s feature register. Build a dedicated composer using existing original/authorized structural campaign commands, without claiming generated creative is finished. Add actor/tenant-bound request idempotency behind one wrapper RPC and an unexposed private table. `ActionForm.preserveValues` opts the composer out of React’s automatic form reset so failed commands retain controlled selections. Local-date conversion uses the server-authorized tenant timezone, rejects nonexistent DST times and requires explicit ambiguity resolution. Preserve approvals/provider gates. Import only clean-room requirement descriptions into the repository register, never legacy code, prompts or runtime content. This branch is stacked on the still-open onboarding PR #2; do not merge or deploy it automatically.

- ONB-001: the user requested immediate implementation with onboarding as the priority. Add a resumable, server-derived first-use guide and reduce visible navigation. Preserve existing authorization, revision checks and fixture boundaries. Workspace creation redirects to `/get-started`; existing users keep their dashboard. Shared auth/navigation presentation and brand action cache invalidation are integration-owned changes in this slice.

- UI-002: the user requested a whole-product review and visual polish. Retain the neutral Inter/graphite/blue identity; improve the shared shell, owner overview and calendar, and fix narrowly evidenced usability defects. Cross-domain presentation and query edits are justified by this integration review: stable scoped collection reads, direct authorized detail reads, and current-tenant active-subscription choices for library/rendering/GROS. Existing action payloads, domain authority, exact approvals, provider gates and migrations remain unchanged. Reuse the existing checkout and preserve the pre-existing local preview/Supabase data. Use a disposable stack for mutating acceptance and record gaps separately from implemented fixture functionality.

- V2-011: domain mutations and worker leases lock the tenant before campaign/job/usage resources; expired reservations are released only without a live lease. GROS rechecks creator visibility after waiting for tenant/conversation locks and uses conversation-locked ordinals and bounded cursor pages; proposals require actual scheduling/assignment readiness before reservation. Plans freeze exact local custom events and canonical pinned-rule versions and reject changed inputs. Operator-only design escalation/resolution persists in campaign audit events with exact current workflow version and verified overdue SLA, without creative approval changes. Cron dispatch requires a secret, validated scope and explicit loopback fake mode; live webhooks return unavailable without ingesting content. Business stale-version/lease conflicts use `PT409` (HTTP 409), preserving checks and ACLs; custom `40001` caused infinite PostgREST 14 retries in the actual concurrent RPC check. Historical migration files stay intact, with the conversion in integration migration `20260914090011`. Source: [Supabase troubleshooting](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/troubleshooting/high-cpu-and-infinite-transaction-retries-when-using-custom-error-codes-in-rpc-functions-77326b).

- V2-010: every fixture sweep requires exact owner preview approval. Twelve calendar months (UTC) of purchaser protection wins over the 180-day order candidate rule; consent, bounce/spam, existing suppression and changed evidence are rechecked at each stage. Two original local winback audits precede ten-day nonresponse suppression. Concurrent active sweeps are rejected. Reversal removes only the optimizer reason when all consent/protected-reason checks pass. Dated fictional band pricing is an estimate with no measured savings. Own/reference reuse copies structural metadata into an unapproved original draft; cross-client publication remains off.

- V2-009: provider result contracts include explicit window bounds, completeness/comparability, currency and confounders. Send metadata freezes with the immutable package before provider creation. Learning uses complete seven-day same-cohort sends within thirty days, five per subject-length side; only owner-pinned preferences influence matching fixture copy and retain evidence lineage. Re-synthesis never changes dismissal. Ten consecutive first-time confirmed synthetic sends is the default L2 streak; any owner rejection resets its generation. L2 simulation is actor/policy/brand/version-bound at 72 hours; production L2 and design delegation remain constrained off.

- V2-008: GROS visibility is checked by RLS and every mutation; a shared conversation never transfers proposal authority. Package cycles retain immutable definitions, atomic synthetic usage is reserved before generation, and actual fixture usage is reconciled by operation. Verified-email invitation acceptance is derived from local Auth. Operator diagnostics contain metadata/counts only. The shared worker helper now treats a missing JWT role as denied, avoiding nullable-boolean authorization.

- V2-007: immutable provider schedule precision is milliseconds, matching the package ISO snapshot. L1 is an explicit revisioned owner policy; it does not grant general chat authority. Every provider mutation requires server-recorded conclusive reconciliation, not just a worker convention. Rescheduling retains provider identity while adding a new immutable package; rejected cancellation retains actual remote state. Only an explicit labelled simulated send creates a private own skeleton. Sent snapshots remain immutable during later asset/kit corrections. The live Klaviyo class remains disabled: public campaign/send-job contracts were checked, but hosted artifact, account acceptance and transport reconciliation are unverified.

- V2-006: deterministic fixture design artifacts use trusted, self-contained SVG strips in a private server-write-only bucket; text-email HTML remains readable without slicing. This needs no rendering dependency. SVG email-provider/client compatibility and aesthetic quality are externally unverified; live delivery stays disabled. Package assembly is rebuilt from approved sources, never caller HTML. Per-strip revisions retain unchanged content-addressed artifacts, and own templates remain tenant private and originate only from confirmed simulated sends.

| Decision            | Value                                                                                          | Reason                                                                                                     |
| ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Runtime             | Next.js App Router + TypeScript                                                                | One deployable web application with server-rendered role surfaces.                                         |
| Architecture        | Modular monolith with explicit domain modules                                                  | Fast MVP iteration without premature distributed-system overhead.                                          |
| Hosting             | Vercel                                                                                         | Preview deployments and server functions.                                                                  |
| Backend             | Supabase Postgres/Auth/Storage/Realtime/Queues                                                 | Durable relational core and tenant security.                                                               |
| Isolation           | Server authorization plus PostgreSQL RLS                                                       | Deny-by-default tenant boundaries with defense in depth.                                                   |
| Data access         | `@supabase/ssr` plus generated database types and SQL/RPC                                      | Keeps RLS authoritative and avoids a competing schema source.                                              |
| Validation          | Zod                                                                                            | Shared environment, form, action, and adapter validation.                                                  |
| UI                  | Tailwind CSS + accessible headless primitives                                                  | Fast, consistent, responsive implementation.                                                               |
| Tests               | Vitest + Playwright + pgTAP/SQL security tests                                                 | Covers code, user journeys, and RLS.                                                                       |
| Async               | Durable queue + idempotent workers                                                             | External API work must survive retries and timeouts.                                                       |
| Demo behavior       | Seeded fixtures + fake adapters                                                                | Complete workflow without external secrets or billable calls.                                              |
| Dates               | Store UTC; render in tenant IANA timezone                                                      | Removes the timezone contradiction found in the reference product.                                         |
| Money               | Integer micro-USD internally                                                                   | Avoids floating-point cost errors.                                                                         |
| IDs                 | UUID primary keys; external IDs in unique scoped columns                                       | Stable internal identity and provider reconciliation.                                                      |
| Visual system       | Cool neutral field, white panels, graphite actions, blue interaction cues                      | Matches the user-selected reference theme through original semantic tokens.                                |
| Design review       | Brand owner reviews/revises and approves the exact current design version                      | User-confirmed correction; admins handle assignment, SLA, and escalation.                                  |
| v2 build scope      | New implementation on existing foundation; email-first fake/fixture execution                  | Autonomous plan in `docs/OVERNIGHT_BUILD_PLAN.md`; external actions remain separately authorization-gated. |
| Approval / delivery | Revision-bound approvals; immutable package; reconcile unknown provider outcomes               | Aligns diagrams and target contracts; V2-000 owns code/type/migration integration.                         |
| Autopilot default   | L0 owner confirms; L1 standing policy after owner approvals; production L2/delegation disabled | Preserves owner design control while permitting simulated background scheduling.                           |

Confirmed: reference-neutral visual direction, white-label-first architecture, code-first build path, DRY shared implementation. The selected Impeccable direction uses seed `6f728f1c`; its durable contract is `DESIGN.md` plus `.impeccable/design.json`.

- SHP-001 / V2-D2 (2026-11-07): v2 is Klaviyo only (BUILD_BRIEF D2, VISION rule 1). Connections shows the Klaviyo card only; the Shopify card, form and `connectShopify` action are gone (the credential helpers, verifier, `shopify-read` port, `store-data` reader, `features/store` dashboard and the tables stay, unused, with no destructive migration). `/store` redirects to `/brand` and the Brand & Store tab row is not shown (one tab left). The Dashboard's UTM tile no longer asks to connect Shopify. Empty product lists say products will come from the Klaviyo catalog (the live catalog is V2-S3a, #310). The Sample data tools keep the product fixture, which still uses the internal `shopify` connection row for its `commerce.sync` job, but call it "sample catalog" on screen. The brand-mismatch guard keeps its Shopify checks; they never fire without a Shopify connection.

- RET-002 (2026-11-11): decisions D16 and D17. No weekly approval: `/copy-review` (the old weekly copy batch) answers 404 through the proxy like the RET-001 designer routes, and nothing links to it; APR-001/APR-002 code (`features/approval/`) stays unrouted and unscheduled. No human loop: `withCopyQa` answers `fallback` instead of `needs_human`; callers record one entry in `public.agent_fallbacks` through `public.record_fallback` (service role only; one row per tenant and dedupe key, a repeat counts `occurrences` and keeps the latest reason) using `copyCheckFallback` / `assemblyFallback` (`features/fallback/entry.ts`) and a `FallbackLog` (`features/fallback/log.ts`: database and memory versions; writing never throws). Platform admins read it on Admin → Operations through `public.admin_fallback_log(max_rows)`. Agents use short keys (`copy_check`, `assembly`, later `brand_inspector`, `section_tagger` …), so new agents need no migration. Lane B's `ops_tasks` (inbox) are unchanged.

- STR-001 (2026-11-11): the Brand pulse (`features/strategy/pulse.ts`). Code builds the evidence (each fact with an id and its words) and decides what may be offered: only real codes, valid on the window's first day, at or under the max discount (others are listed as left out with the reason). The agent (`brand-pulse`, purpose `review` = Flash, as AGENT_MAP's model table says; the Brief writer and Calendar planner will use `plan`) only picks evidence ids and says why, without numbers, codes or dates; `checkPulseAnswer` enforces that and out-of-stock products. Up to 3 tries with the exact problems; a refused call (cap, provider) is not retried; then `codePulse` and one fallback entry. The owner's pick is `OfferPick` (`auto`, `none`, `picked` offer ids from the pulse only); STR-003 stores it with the plan run. Sample store in `features/strategy/sample.ts` until lane B's readers land. After review: `readPulseInput` checks the store data (a bad brand or start throws; a bad row is left out and counted); `until` is the code's real last day, inclusive in store time (copy QA's `expiresAt` must be the end of that day); out-of-stock products are never launch or bestseller facts; `statedFact` (`features/strategy/words.ts`) refuses digits, money signs, number words, promise words ("free shipping"), weekdays, months and the store's codes as whole words; `tryAgent` (`features/strategy/retry.ts`) is the one retry loop for every agent.

- STR-002 (2026-11-11): the Competitor mapper (`features/strategy/competitors.ts`). Code keeps library emails of the same industry and size band from the last 90 days, places each on our 15 days (the same number of days, 0–7, before a date of the same kind: holiday, sale or event; or 0–6 days before month end when our month end is in the window), and groups them into patterns counted by distinct brands (at least 2). The agent (`competitor-mapper`, purpose `review`) sees only pattern ids, timing, types, offers, angles and counts (no email ids, brand ids, names or words), picks patterns and names each move without numbers or quotes; code writes "2 days before Public holiday: 3 of 5 similar brands. …" and the confidence (brand share). Same 3-try check and fallback as the pulse. `LibraryEmail` is the shape CI-001's Email classifier should feed. After review: its `industry`, `brandSize`, `emailType`, `offerType` and `angle` come from lane B's closed lists in `features/intelligence/taxonomy.ts` (CI-001, imported, not copied), so no competitor name, subject or phrase can ride in a label; untagged emails (null labels) are left out; rows that do not match (or have a broken date) are left out, and `readMapperInput` refuses a broken start.

- STR-003 part 1 (2026-11-11): `planFifteenDays` (`features/strategy/plan.ts`). Brief writer (`brief-writer`, `plan`) writes up to 8 actions (goal, fact ids from the pulse and the competitor moves, a date range in the 15 days, an allowed offer or none); Calendar planner (`calendar-planner`, `plan`) turns them into up to 10 slots (action, date, local time, one of 8 email types, angle, up to 3 products, offer, audience); the calendar rules (`calendarProblems`, code) refuse days outside the window or the action, no-email days, unknown or out-of-stock products, offers not allowed or already ended, unknown audiences, numbers in angles, more than the owner's sends per week, two emails to one audience on a day, and, with the shared planning policy, offers above 40% of a week's emails and offers closer than 7 days; the Strategy critic (`strategy-critic`, `review`) adds its own problems. Up to 2 rounds give the planner both lists; whatever still fails is dropped and logged. Agents that cannot answer fall back to `codeBrief` / `codeSlots` (logged); a critic that cannot answer leaves the rules alone (logged). Facts, prices and codes in the plan come from code, never from an agent. After review: `readPlanInput` refuses a pulse for other days, bad dates or send times, and keeps only the pulse's own offers; the kept plan is re-checked after every drop (`keepPassing`), so dropping an email can never leave a week above the offer share; the planner gets its previous plan with slot indexes and every problem when revising, the round with the fewest failing slots is kept, and a planner refused while revising keeps its best plan; the critic has the usual 3 tries; rules have stable keys (`CalendarRule`), new ones tie offers to offer emails, keep send times in 07:00–21:00 and refuse products before their launch day; `existing` covers the 7 days before the window too; drops are logged under `calendar-rules` (with the rule keys) or `strategy-critic`; the planner sees at most 30 products; with 2 emails a week no offer fits, and the plan says so in `notes`.

- EML-001 (2026-11-11): layer 1 of the email build (`features/email-build/sections.ts`). `BuildMode` is `copy` (D1 Exact: the competitor's tagged sections through ASM-003's `layoutSequence`, code only), `remix` (the competitor's angle as a structure-only `strategyNote`) or `scratch`. `Variant` (`id`, `family` = one of the 20 block types, `industries`, `emailTypes`, `score`) is the library shape CI-002's Variant maker should feed; until then `seedVariants` has one `<family>/seed` per type. `searchVariants` keeps allowed families for the industry and email type, best results first, 3 per family. The `section-planner` agent (purpose `copy`, Flash) picks variants by id; `checkSections` requires real variants of allowed families, the logo header first (after an optional announcement bar), the footer last, one button, one hero, products within the slot's count and an offer banner only with an offer. Then the usual 3 tries and a logged fallback to a safe layout per email type (`codeSectionPlan`), which always passes the check. `maxOutputTokens` is the answer's room only: MEM-002 (#370) adds the Gemini 2.5 thinking budget in the provider.
- EML-002 (2026-11-11): layer 2 of the email build (`features/email-build/content.ts`). Code locks each value from its source: products from the catalog (`productLock`: in the catalog, featurable, https photo, fits a product section, a known price; a repeated id counts once, the first), the slot's offer from the discount codes (`offerLock`: on the list, within the max, still working at send time; the percent and end come from the code, the end shown in store time), pictures from the catalog or the confirmed photos, links by `storeLink`'s rule. What cannot be locked becomes a safer choice: another product, no offer (also when no offer section is left, so the writer never gets a code it cannot show), a smaller product section (`product_grid_3col` → `product_grid_2col` → `product_feature`), a product hero for a picture hero without a photo, or no section; replaced sections use the family's seed variant and the email always keeps a message. At most 6 products are locked (`maxLocked`, the job boundary's `focusProductIds` limit). The `content-locker` agent (purpose `copy`, Flash, no thinking) only places product ids into the product sections, and only when there is a choice; `checkPlacement` enforces exact counts, known ids, each once, the plan's main product first and planned products before others; 3 tries, then plan order (logged). Changes are logged once per email as `content_replaced` with fixed words (`changeWords`, `didWords`) and the change keys. Contracts: `LockedContent.input` is the narrowed `AssemblyInput` (only the locked products in section order as `focusProductIds`, only the locked code as `offerCode`); `assembleLocked` (`features/assembly/modes.ts`) is the Copywriter on exactly the locked sections, so each locked product lands in its locked section (tested from the lock to the written email). Shared changes in ASM-001/003: `productTakes` is the one product-placement rule (`fill` and the sample writer): a grid shows 4 or 6 only when the later product sections still get their fewest (`minProducts`, also used by the Section planner); `layoutSequence` budgets the fewest products per section; `storeLink` uses a store link only when it is a real link, the first product's page only when there is none, and no link sections when it is broken.
- EML-003 (2026-11-11): the end of layer 2 (`features/email-build/inspect.ts`). `writeAndInspect` runs the Copywriter on the locked sections (`assembleLocked`), then code checks first, every round: assembly's own problems, copy QA (`checkCopy`, ASM-002) on the locked facts and code, a lock check (the draft shows exactly the locked products and code), `priceChecks` (a money amount the writer writes must be exactly a locked product's price) and `emailChecks` (subject at most 60 characters, at most one exclamation mark and no capitals words, preview 20 to 140 characters and not the subject, `spamPhrases`, no web or email address typed into the words, one button section, every link one the Content locker locked, alt text on every picture, at most 300 words). The checks read only the writer's words: `protectedText` masks product names, the brand name, the code, its percent and end, reviews, the founder's note and photo descriptions, so the writer is never asked to change what it cannot. Code is the final word: only a draft that passes code goes to the `brand-inspector` (purpose `review`, reads the Brand book and do's and don'ts) and the `email-inspector` (purpose `label`); each returns problems with a field, an exact quote of the writer's own words (`checkInspection` refuses store words), a reason and a fix; a draft seen before reuses its verdict. Feedback carries the last two rounds' reasons. The first write plus `rewriteRounds` (3) rewrites; then plain copy (`assembleLockedPlain`: facts-only words that never say "new", subject within 60, `Draft.source` `plain`), which must pass the same code checks, else nothing is built. Writer errors: busy or down (`rate_limited`, `unavailable`, `timeout`, `ledger_unavailable`) returns `retry` for the job; no writer (`cap_reached`, set-up or account problems, refusals) and the fake writer go to plain copy with their `cause` (`allowance_used`, `no_live_writer`; plain copy from the fake is sample-mode copy and must be treated like ASM-001's sample drafts); a wrong-shape answer is a round with its exact problems; `AssemblyError` and bugs throw. One fallback entry per email (`copy-inspectors`, `copy_plain_fallback` or `copy_failed`) with the cause and only code rule keys as candidate don'ts for LRN-002; an inspector that cannot answer is logged once and skipped. Contracts: `Draft.source` gains `plain` (ASM-001); `writerRules` gains the inbox limits.
- STR-003 part 2 (2026-11-11): "Plan next 15 days" end to end. Migration `strategy_runs` (number at merge, D24): one open run per workspace (`pulse_queued` → `offers_ready` → `plan_queued` → `planned`, or `failed`), starting tomorrow in the store's timezone; owner commands `start_strategy_run(t)` (returns the open run, or replaces one whose days have begun, closing it as `out_of_date`) and `pick_strategy_offers(t, run, pick)` (`auto`, `none`, or `picked` offer ids the pulse lists; PT409 when the run is not waiting or its days have begun); service-role job commands `claim_strategy_run(target)` (one run or the oldest waiting, active workspaces only, 5-minute lease, skip locked, a failed step waits a minute, a step out of 3 attempts is failed and the claim looks on), `finish_strategy_pulse`, `finish_strategy_plan` and `fail_strategy_step(run, lease, code, final)` (exact leases; `final` fails at once when a retry cannot help). Even the service role writes only through the commands. **Cron contract change:** the sweep (`/api/internal/jobs`) now runs plan steps for at most 5 s right after the inbox step, before the workspace jobs, and reports `plans: { ran }`. This is safe because `runStrategyRuns` (`features/strategy/worker.ts`) runs only the code-only path while store data is the sample store (no AI call, nothing metered, nothing logged as a fallback); the AI agents are wired into the job with the first real store reader, with their own time limit and a lease long enough for paid steps. The owner's click runs his own run right after the reply (`after()`). Saved pulses and plans are re-checked on read (`features/strategy/stored.ts`) by the screen and the job. Store data comes through the `StrategyData` port, which says when it is the sample store; the screen says so plainly. The `/plan` screen (sidebar: Plan next 15 days) starts a plan, shows progress, the pulse and the offer pick (ticked offers with "you choose" or "no offers" are refused, never dropped), then every planned email with its reason and facts; staff see it read-only; a plan whose days have begun offers "Start again".
- EML-004 part 1 (2026-11-11): layer 3 of the email build (`features/email-build/design.ts`). Approach A (`designA`) renders the draft's blocks with the BLK-001 renderer; approach B (`designB`) swaps the first `hero_product` picture for a designed one from `HeroImagePort` (made from the real product photo; `designedPictureOk`: plain https, no `data:`, no user or password). Each look takes desktop (600px) and 375px screenshots through `ScreenshotPort`; code checks first (`no_screenshot`, `broken_images`, `phone_overflow`), and only a clean look goes to the `visual-inspector` (purpose `review`, Brand book): for B it also gets the original product photo and the designed section's number, and must report any words, numbers, prices, codes or logos in the picture or a changed product (VISION rules 9 and 10). B is used only when the inspector looked and found nothing in the picture's section or the whole email; problems elsewhere are shared with A and cost no new picture. B gets `designTries` (3) tries, told fixed words per problem (`pictureReasons`, never the inspector's own text); a picture that cannot be made, a look that could not be taken or judged (`not_inspected`), or 3 failed tries fall back to A with one `design_b_fallback` entry from `designer-b` (rule keys only). A is the last word: its findings are returned and logged (`design_a_findings`, `designer-a`) for the launch check and LRN-002. Adapters: only fakes exist (`fakeScreenshots`; `fakeHeroImages` returns the photo, so on fakes B looks like A). Port contract for the live picture maker: reserve its cost against the client's AI allowance through the AI ledger with `tenantId` and `callerKey`, no words in the picture, upload to Klaviyo (VISION rule 11) and return that https link, own timeout. Before live use: a screenshot browser job like INB-002's, the Gemini 2.5 Flash Image adapter with its price in `lib/providers/gemini-pricing.ts`, and the owner's OK.
- EML-004 part 2 (2026-11-11): "Create email" end to end (D17). Migration `email_builds` (number at merge, D24): one build per planned email at a time (`build_queued` → `push_queued` → `drafted`, or `failed`) and one `push_key` per planned email: a new build after a failed one that reached the push keeps its key, so "Try again" never makes a second Klaviyo draft (blueprint: unknown remote outcomes reconcile before a repeat). The owner's `create_email_build(t, run, slot)` (owner only, tenant locked first, the latest plan only, the run planned, the email's day not passed in the workspace's timezone; clicking again returns the open or drafted build); service-role `claim_email_build` (pushes first, 5-minute lease; brings the planned email, the push key, the workspace timezone and the plan's first day; a failed step waits a minute; 3 attempts), `finish_email_build`, `finish_email_push`, `fail_email_step(code, final, retry_after_seconds)` (a busy Klaviyo's wait is kept in `not_before` and does not use up an attempt, at most 20 waits). `buildEmail` is the code-only path for now, like STR-003 part 2: the safe section layout, products placed in plan order by the Content locker (`agent: false`), plain facts-only copy that must pass every code check, coded design A; no AI call. It builds in the workspace's one brand timezone (`tenants.timezone`), reads offers through DSC-001's `OfferSource` at the send time (`offersAt`: no answer or no max means no discount), and the sample store starts from the plan's first day. Offers (supervisor, 11 Nov): `ownerOrSampleOffers` reads `SupabaseOfferSource.managed` with the job's service-role client; a saved max (a `brand_offers` row) or any active code wins, even while the store is the sample store; only when the owner saved nothing do the sample offers from the plan's first day apply; a failed read gives no discount, never the sample. Limits: after 5 failed builds a planned email is not built again (PT409); a step whose planned day has passed (store time) fails as `day_passed` when claimed; the push step gets its own attempts and waits; a built email needs a subject. A product is unfeaturable only when its stock is known to be 0 (`featurableStock`): unknown stock is allowed. `runEmailBuilds` checks the built email's shape and size before storing it, fails refused finishes at once, and pushes through `DraftPusher` with the push key; the owner's click runs both steps right after the reply, the cron sweep runs up to 10 waiting steps in 5 s. `DraftPusher` (`lib/providers/klaviyo-draft.ts`, contract agreed with the supervisor): draft only, never schedule or send; one idempotency key per planned email, and the same key updates that draft to the email just built (a draft the owner already scheduled or sent is refused as `rejected`, never changed); never throws, failures as `auth`, `rate_limited` with `retryAfterSeconds`, `unavailable`, `rejected` with the failing launch checks; it uploads the pictures and runs the launch check before creating the draft; a `sample` email is refused by a live adapter; PSH-001 builds it, `fakeDraftPusher` until then. `StoreSource` (`features/email-build/ports.ts`) is sample until lane B's readers. The plan shows each email's state ("Creating…", "Putting it into Klaviyo…", "Sample draft (not in Klaviyo)" or "In Klaviyo as a draft", or the failure's reason with "Try again"); no button for a day that has passed. `/plan/email/[buildId]` shows the built email through the safe preview (`previewHtml`, `ScaledFrame`) with plain notes.

## Migration allocation

To reduce parallel conflicts, each workstream owns a numeric migration block. Do not renumber an already shared migration.

| Range            | Owner                                |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| `2026091400xxxx` | Foundation/extensions/helpers        |
| `2026091401xxxx` | Identity/tenancy/RLS                 |
| `2026091402xxxx` | Brand/AI/prompts/usage               |
| `2026091403xxxx` | Integrations/jobs/Klaviyo/Shopify    |
| `2026091404xxxx` | Campaigns/calendar/audiences         |
| `2026091405xxxx` | References/competitors/templates     |
| `2026091406xxxx` | Designers/submissions                |
| `2026091407xxxx` | Support/feedback/notifications       |
| `2026091408xxxx` | Packages/reporting/admin additions   |
| `2026091409xxxx` | Integration fixes/materialized views |

New migrations after the hosted project reached `20260914090016` use the next `2026091409xxxx` number, whatever their domain. Since 2026-11-06 the Supabase GitHub integration applies every migration merged to `main` to the live database, and the hosted project refuses out-of-order versions. **Since 2026-11-11 (D24) there are no reservations:** right before merging, rename an unmerged migration to the next number after the newest on `main`, then re-run `test:db`. On `main`: `090017` (UIR-013 connection profiles), `090018`–`090019` (UIR-005 brand profile sections and facts), `090020`–`090021` (UIR-011 Settings), `090022`–`090023` (UIR-012 Holidays), `090024`–`090025` (UIR-019 Team), `090026`–`090027` (UIR-020 Feature Requests), `090028`–`090030` (UIR-023 brand timezone), `090031`–`090032` (UIR-021 brand-mismatch guard), `090033`–`090034` (UIR-009 Inspiration), `090038`–`090039` (INB-001 inbox), `090040` (INB-002 images and screenshots), `090043` (INB-003 sections and facts), `090044`–`090045` (INB-004 ops queue and brand directory), `090046` (GEM-002 live AI usage), `090047` (GEM-003 platform AI budget), `090048` (MEM-001 agent memory), `090049` (KLV-001 live Klaviyo), `090050` (CI-001 competitor tags), `090051` (RET-002 agent fallbacks), `090052` (CI-002 block variants), `090054` (DSC-001 max discount and discount codes), `090055` (ONB-101 brand findings), `090056` (STR-003 part 2 strategy runs), `090057` (ONB-102 catalog reading), `090058` (EML-004 part 2 email builds), `090059` (ONB-103 Klaviyo history; numbered at merge time). `090035`–`090036` (UIR-008 Ask GROS, parked) are older than `main` and must be renumbered before they merge.

Use a valid 14-digit Supabase migration version inside the assigned UTC-hour namespace, for example `20260914010000_create_tenants.sql`, then increment seconds monotonically. The namespace is an ordering device, not a claim about wall-clock creation time.

No numbers are reserved (D24): GEM-002 and GEM-003 merged as `090046` and `090047`; the `090060`/`090061` reservation they once had is void.

## Release checklist

- [ ] Database resets from zero with migrations and safe seed.
- [ ] Generated Supabase types match the final schema.
- [ ] Typecheck, lint, unit, integration, RLS, and Playwright suites pass.
- [ ] Client/admin/designer authorization is deny-by-default.
- [ ] Two-tenant negative-access suite passes.
- [ ] Service-role key never appears in a client bundle.
- [ ] External credentials are encrypted or held only in platform secrets.
- [ ] Queue consumers use leases, attempts, idempotency keys, and dead-letter behavior.
- [ ] Cron endpoints validate a secret and do not trust public callers.
- [ ] AI usage checks run before provider dispatch and reconcile actual usage afterward.
- [ ] File buckets and signed URLs enforce tenant/assignment access.
- [ ] Security headers and rate limits are enabled.
- [ ] Production contains no demo passwords or destructive seed behavior.
- [ ] Preview smoke test completes the P0 campaign journey.
- [ ] Rollback and operational runbooks are documented.

In the preceding run (58/64), all 8 pasted-reference journeys passed (desktop/mobile: hostile paste with no external requests, failed-save retention, idempotent replay, provenance and reopening the same campaign; two-workspace/foreign isolation; long-paste limit; staff denial), as did all composer/onboarding journeys and all 32 desktop journeys.

(2) Targeted rerun: one Chromium launch timeout (environmental) and a wrong assertion of mine (decoded entity text legitimately contains `<`), corrected without weakening. (3) First full run 55/62: seven consecutive mobile failures starting with a planning timeout and `net::ERR_NETWORK_IO_SUSPENDED` (host network suspension). (4) Clean full run 61/62: desktop statistics missed the "· 7d" window. Diagnosed from persisted job history: each reporting request drains at most four jobs ordered by `available_at, created_at`; jobs left by earlier journeys in the shared tenant are claimed first, and the 48h/7d pulls share one timestamp, so one can miss the batch. The test now waits for the clock advance to persist and drains via the existing "Run reporting fixture jobs" control; all assertions kept. (5) A third full run was interrupted by the supervisor at 46/46 passing. (6) A scheduling+statistics subset was abandoned: scheduling depends on planning/rendering output. (7) Full run 58/64. Six mobile failures came from a pre-existing race in `tests/e2e/planning.spec.ts`: it treated "Approve reviewed calendar plan" reaching count 0 as completion, but that submit button relabels to "Saving…" while pending, so the test opened copy review before the approval persisted. The entries were approved afterward, but the page showed none and the click timed out. Mobile rendering, scheduling, statistics and templates depend on that output and cascaded. With integration-owner authorization, the test now waits for the outer "Review plan approval" control, which renders only while proposed entries exist and unmounts only after persisted approval. Supervisor review agreed. (8) Full run 64/64 (above). (9) Final review fix after that run: preview truncation previously cut between UTF-16 surrogate halves (for example `"a".repeat(19999)` followed by an emoji), producing a lone surrogate that Postgres JSONB rejects, so a valid long paste could not be saved. Truncation is now surrogate-safe (see Global decisions). New unit regressions cover a pair straddling the cutoff, a pair ending exactly at it, exact-bound and short Unicode input, and the action keeping the full original. The long-import browser journey now places an emoji across the bound and verifies save, a well-formed stored preview, the unchanged CRLF original and the reopened notice. Verification for this helper change: `pnpm verify` (format/lint/typecheck, 130 unit tests in 40 files, production build), then on a clean reset local stack only the independent `tests/e2e/pasted-reference.spec.ts`, 8/8 desktop/mobile. The 64-journey full run was not repeated; its result above stands for everything except this helper change. No SQL, migration or type changes. Supervisor verification also reran `node scripts/test-first-draft.mjs`: 12 concurrent requests returned one unapproved saved draft and changed-input replay was rejected.
- Known issues: no remaining verification failure in this task. Product limits: paste only, so no HTML/EML/screenshot upload, MIME metadata, sandboxed visual preview, AI analysis, rebranding or visual reconstruction. The outline is a paragraph count, not layout. Imported outlines are not rendering structures. No delete/edit/version flow. The server body bound counts the CRLF-expanded text, so a paste at exactly the client limit with many line breaks may be rejected as too long. A preview cut through an astral character ends with an ellipsis instead of that character; the original keeps it. Evidence logs and scripts are in ignored `coverage/ref-002/`.

### 2026-11-06 — STAB-001 — scheduled job worker

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/stab-001-scheduled-job-worker`, branched from `main` at `9e9d56c` and updated with `main` by merge.
- Commit(s): see the STAB-001 pull request.
- Changed contracts: new `GET /api/internal/jobs` scheduled sweep and `features/cron/scheduler.ts`; `scheduledDispatchAllowed` in `features/cron/gate.ts`. New root `vercel.json` (cron only), `.github/workflows/scheduled-jobs.yml`, and a `scheduled-sweep` project in `playwright.config.ts`. See Global decision STAB-001. Register: EXE-022 (atomic queue claiming) moves to `verified_fixture`.
- Migrations: none.
- Verification run: `pnpm verify` passed (format, lint, typecheck, 311/311 unit tests in 60 files, 21 new: scheduler ordering, empty queue, waiting work outside the active-workspace page, bounded rounds, time budget, no empty round, workspace cap, per-workspace failure isolation, due/stale read failures, excluded paid kinds; route 401 for missing/wrong/non-bearer/unset secret and before any configuration check, 503 for live mode and missing service configuration, safe 500, counts-only body; adapter gate). Fresh local reset → `pnpm test:db` 600/600. Fresh reset → full `pnpm test:e2e` 80/80 in 11.9 minutes on a lean local stack (studio, analytics, vector, imgproxy, edge runtime, realtime, meta and pooler excluded), the two scheduled-sweep journeys last: three simultaneous authorized sweeps finished three queued copy jobs in two workspaces with `attempt = 1` and one generated version each; a paused workspace's job stayed `queued`/`attempt 0`; a repeat sweep changed nothing; a job whose retry wait had passed and a job left `running` with an expired lease both finished on attempt 2; a job whose fourth attempt had an expired lease became `failed` (`stale_lease`) with no fifth try; unauthorized `GET`s returned 401. The workflow's `curl --config -` header passing was checked against a local echo server with a dummy value.
- Review: `code-reviewer` found no security, tenant-isolation, owner-authority or live-provider defects. Its findings were all fixed: the 10-minute schedule used Actions minutes even without a secret (now opt-in through `SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED`); waiting work beyond the first 1,000 active workspaces could be skipped (the active filter is now in the due-job query); retry and stale-lease paths were untested through the sweep (two journeys added); the sweep spec did not really run last (now its own dependent project, full suite re-run); the URL override could send the secret anywhere (removed; HTTPS only); a failed-visit warning was added; round counting and docs were corrected; rotation steps were documented.
- Known issues: GitHub Actions is disabled for the repository, so neither CI nor the 10-minute schedule runs until the owner enables it and sets `SCHEDULED_JOBS_ENABLED`; the daily Vercel cron needs `CRON_SECRET` in Vercel Production. At 10 minutes the schedule uses about 4,400 Actions minutes a month on this private repository (above the free allowance); the runbook lists slower options. AI rewrites and file imports still resume only from the owner's page. No admin last-run status screen yet (OPS-014). Concurrency evidence shows each job ran once while three sweeps were in flight; it does not prove the sweeps' claims interleaved, which the database lock makes serial per workspace anyway.
- Next consumer: owner adds the secrets, enables Actions and the schedule; STAB-002 can then hide the "Run … jobs" buttons from owners.

New tests: subject (entities, invisible titles, emoji cut, hostile input), action (size before parse, limit wording), composer (rewrite off, oversized, link shortcut), setup step for staff, `/create-email` mode handling, `ChooseOutcome` marking without starting work.

### 2026-11-06 — STAB-004 — CI on pull requests

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/stab-004-re-enable-ci-on-pull-requests` from `main` at `f3883c4`.
- Commit(s): see the STAB-004 pull request.
- Changed contracts: CI layout (see Global decision STAB-004). No application code, migration or dependency change.
- Migrations: none.
- Verification run: `tests/ci/workflows.test.ts` 11/11 (verify triggers, steps, exact job id and no job-level condition; a time limit on every job and explicit permissions on every workflow; quoted and unquoted path filters are read and match GitHub-style globs; every filter matches a tracked file; every tracked file is either in the browser filter or on an explicit no-journey-effect list; database filter covers schema, tests and the Supabase CLI version; both Supabase starts use the same verified service list; drafts skip slow checks and `ready_for_review` starts them; a step limit keeps the failure upload reachable and CI writes an HTML report; the scheduled-jobs workflow stays opt-in, HTTPS-only and at most twice an hour). All five workflow files parse as YAML; `pnpm verify` passed. The exact CI sequences were run locally on the lean stack: fresh reset → `pnpm test:db` 600/600; fresh reset → full `pnpm test:e2e` 80/80 (STAB-001 run); fresh reset → `test-first-draft`, `test-email-concurrency`, `test-email-import-concurrency` all passed. The first GitHub-hosted run is this pull request's own checks.
- Review: `code-reviewer` found nothing blocking. Fixed: browser filter now covers `tests/helpers/**` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and narrows `scripts/**` to runtime `scripts/*.mjs`; the failure artifact now has an HTML report (CI reporter `github` + `html`, shared `playwright.config.ts`) and `pnpm test:e2e` has a 30-minute step limit so a slow run fails and still uploads; database filter adds `package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` and also runs after such merges to `main`; tests guard the `verify` job id, unquoted filters and `ready_for_review`; runs on `main` are no longer cancelled by later merges; the low-value Playwright cache was removed; workflow comments warn not to make path-filtered checks required.
- Known issues: GitHub Actions is still disabled, so no GitHub-hosted run has been observed; first runs on private-repo 2-core runners may be slower than local (browser time limit 40 minutes). Branch protection can only be set by the owner (steps in `docs/CI_RUNBOOK.md`). Docs-only pull requests show only `verify`. The browser job no longer runs on push to `main`, so a broken merge result between two green pull requests would only be caught by `verify` or the next pull request.
- Owner update: Actions was enabled on November 6, 2026; at the owner's request this PR also moves the scheduled-jobs workflow to every 30 minutes and updates both runbooks.
- Next consumer: owner enables Actions and makes `verify` required; every later pull request.

### 2026-11-06 — UIR-014 — old-app rebuild §14 Profile

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/uir-014-14-profile` (issue #250) from `main` at `53f002c`, updated with `main` by merge.
- Commit(s): see the UIR-014 pull request.
- Changed contracts: new `features/profile` (server actions with per-field errors and a summary, client forms, `server-only` page loader, shared password rules) and a thin `/profile`. Profile details save through the existing `profiles_update_self` RLS policy and column grants (name, job title, phone). The password change checks the session first, verifies the current password with `createStatelessClient()` (new `lib/supabase/stateless.ts`: anon key, session in memory only, signed out after use), then calls `auth.updateUser`. Rules: password 12+ characters like the reset form (the spec's "8" is raised on purpose), at most 72 UTF-8 bytes (Auth's limit; no `maxLength` that cuts pasted passwords); name 2–120 characters; phone digits, spaces and + ( ) - . with at least one digit. Errors: only `invalid_credentials` means a wrong current password; 429 says "too many attempts"; `weak_password` and `same_password` show at the field; anything else changes nothing and says so. Shell: `ShellNavigation` gains optional `profileHref`; only the client navigation sets it (`/profile`), so admins and designers no longer see a link to a page they cannot open. The account menu and the mobile drawer close after the click. `proxy.ts` now refreshes sessions on `/profile`. Register: ACC-013 `verified_fixture`. Spec §14: all 3 boxes ticked; owner brief §16 fix done (email help links "support" to /support).
- Migrations: none.
- Verification run: `pnpm verify`; `features/profile` and `components/shell` 58/58 (save own row only after the request-context check, redirect stops the write, empty optional fields cleared, inline errors with a summary, phone without digits or with line breaks refused, refused save, demo and failed user check refused; password: throwaway session signed out on every path, wrong current password, rate limit and outage are not called a wrong password, another account's password, 12-character minimum, 72-byte maximum with emoji, mismatch, reuse, weak and reused passwords from Auth at the field, failed update keeps the old password; forms: locked email with support link, errors linked to fields and focused, typed values kept after an error; shell: profile link per surface, menu and drawer close after following it). `supabase/tests/profile_update.test.sql` 7/7 (own row updated, teammate row unchanged, status and other columns refused, inactive profile refused, anon refused). Fresh reset → profile journeys (fresh owner: menu or drawer link and both close, save and reload details, wrong current password, mismatch, short password, real change, old password refused, new password signs in; 375px keyboard order) and workspace-ui journeys on desktop and mobile.
- Review: code-reviewer found admin/designer links to a client-only page and menus left open (blocking), plus proxy matcher, byte limit, error mapping, thin route and test gaps; all fixed as above.
- Known issues: the current-password check guards the form but is not a security boundary while Supabase "secure password change" is off (`supabase/config.toml`; hosted setting not verified): a holder of the session could call Auth directly. Turning it on needs reauthentication handling (follow-up). Phone numbers are visible to teammates (existing RLS). Length limits are app-side only (no database checks; IAM follow-up). Admin and designer surfaces have no profile page yet.
- Next consumer: none.

### 2026-11-07 — GEM-001 — Gemini adapter behind the AI port

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/gem-001-gemini-adapter`
- Commit(s): see the GEM-001 pull request.
- Changed contracts: new `lib/providers/gemini.ts` (`GeminiProvider` implements `AiProvider`), `lib/providers/gemini-pricing.ts` (price table; only priced models can be configured), `lib/providers/bounded-read.ts` (bounded body reader, token and Retry-After helpers). New `features/ai/run.ts` `runAi()` is the single entry point for v2 AI calls: picks the provider (`features/ai/setup.ts`), reserves the upper-bound cost, calls, settles the real cost. `features/ai/budget-ledger.ts` has the `BudgetLedger` port, an in-memory double and `SupabaseBudgetLedger` over the existing service-role RPCs `reserve_fixture_usage` / `reconcile_fixture_usage` (the real package allowance). `GEMINI_API_KEY` added to the server env schema and `.env.example`.
- Migrations: none.
- Verification run: `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck` (after `next typegen`), `pnpm test` 134 files / 964 tests. `pnpm build` could not fetch Google Fonts in the sandbox; CI builds it.
- Known issues: provider choice: `AI_DEFAULT_PROVIDER=fake` (any case) forces the fake; otherwise only `GEMINI_API_KEY` turns Gemini on (the older `GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_KEY` is ignored on purpose). If Vercel sets `AI_DEFAULT_PROVIDER=fake`, remove it to go live. Code review fixes applied: every call is its own reservation (`ai:<purpose>:<callerKey>:<uuid>`, never a raw job id, so the `job_usage_completion` trigger can't settle it) and is settled exactly once, capped at the estimate; inputs are validated; only the allowance message maps to `cap_reached`. **GEM-002 (follow-up, needs a migration after lane B's pending migrations merge):** `reserve_fixture_usage` / `reconcile_fixture_usage` are also granted to `authenticated` owners, so an owner could release a live reservation; and `reserve_budget` labels all non-`email-rewrite:` usage as "fixture" in alerts. Add service-role-only live usage RPCs, true alert text for `ai:` operations, and `PT409` instead of `40001` in `reconcile_budget`. No consumer may use live Gemini in production before GEM-002 merges.
- Next consumer: lane A, BLK-003 (labelling harness, purpose `label`) and ASM-001 (copy, purpose `copy`): call `runAi({ tenantId, callerKey, purpose, request }, { ledger: new SupabaseBudgetLedger(createServiceClient()) })` after authorising the tenant, and store the result before marking the job done.

  - MIME: multipart, quoted-printable, base64, ISO-8859-1, encoded words, HTML-only text, refusals, NUL and half-emoji text made safe, hostile HTML (2.4 MB of unclosed tags) read in well under 3 s;
  - **a recorded Gmail IMAP session**: the real imapflow client against a local server replaying Gmail's answers (AUTHENTICATE PLAIN, SELECT, UID SEARCH, UID FETCH with X-GM-MSGID, UID STORE, LOGOUT). It checks that only `BODY.PEEK[]<0.10485761>` is fetched, only `\Seen` is ever added, nothing is deleted, moved, copied or expunged, the password never travels outside the SASL response, `13:*` answered with 12 means nothing new, a UIDVALIDITY change starts over, a refused login gives `auth` with no server text, and a dropped connection gives a retryable error without crashing;
  - IMAP stand-in: error listener, lock failure logs out, a refused search or missing INBOX is an error (not "nothing new");
  - config: sample inbox by default, Gmail once both values are set, never on a local database unless asked, `INBOX_TRANSPORT=fake` override, half-configured is an error in words;
  - worker: stores, records and assigns once, cursor per message, duplicates, oversized and unreadable messages, a message bigger than the server said, a message the database refuses (kept as `parse_failed`, the cursor moves on), an HTML copy over the bucket limit (raw kept only), storage refusal, lost lease (named, and what was done is still marked read), stored copy missing, failed login, paused and busy, time budget;
  - step: not set up, never throws, stops waiting after 15 s;
  - jobs route: inbox first; inbox read but no workspace job in live mode; workspace jobs get what is left of the budget.
  - browser: the STAB-001 sweep journeys (`tests/e2e/scheduled-sweep.spec.ts`) now expect the `inbox` result: the sample inbox, ready, and of three sweeps at once at least one polls while the others find it busy. Both pass locally.

  pgTAP 1184/1184 on a fresh reset incl. `inbox_capture` 79:
  - grants for anon, members and the worker; private helpers closed; no browser writes on any inbox table (incl. truncate); private bucket;
  - admin-only brands; a `.example` brand is a sample; one alias and one sender domain per brand (PT409, nothing moved); domain, alias and sender checks in words;
  - the sample/real check constraints;
  - one poll per mailbox; own-mailbox claim; stored copy required;
  - alias, sender (subdomain), alias from a registered sender, alias from an unregistered sender (task, also for a forged 251-character sender domain), real mail never in a sample brand and sample mail never in a real one;
  - duplicates; escaped preview; the brand goes live;
  - unknown sender task counted once; parse failure and oversized tasks; path and lease checks;
  - cursor forward only and reset; finish once; retry backoff; a failed login pauses and opens one task; a good login lifts the pause and closes it;
  - an expired lease is taken over, and on the 4th attempt fails with a task; another job kind cannot move the cursor;
  - members read nothing (incl. real brands, real emails and raw files) and write nothing; admins read the inbox but not raw files, and cannot change jobs or tasks from the browser.

- Review: rls-migration-reviewer said "not ready", with one blocker: real mail could reach a sample brand that workspaces read. Fixed by the sample/real rule above. Its should-fix items are done (table revokes, an expired final lease opens a task, skip-locked recovery, sender domains cannot move between brands, racing saves give PT409, one bad message no longer stops the inbox, the test gaps). Its nits are done too: the cursor needs an inbox job, a good poll lifts the pause, admin inputs are checked in words, and the real inbox address is out of code and comments. code-reviewer said "not ready":
  - its four blockers are fixed: an `error` listener on ImapFlow; one bad message is kept and skipped; a linear HTML scan; one time budget for the sweep;
  - its should-fix items are done: `live` stays about providers and the inbox reports its transport and `synthetic`; PT409 codes are named; a `fail_platform_job` error is logged; read marks are set in `finally`; senders are checked against the alias; capped download; the tests;
  - kept as is: `INBOX_TRANSPORT` stays a free string in `lib/env/server.ts`, so a typo only stops the inbox (with a reason) and not the whole app.

  Second pass: rls-migration-reviewer "ready" (blocker closed) after two should-fix items, both done: the sender-mismatch task key hashes the sender (a forged domain could pass the 300-character key limit and stall the inbox), and two admins saving the same new sender domain at once now gives PT409 instead of a silent loss. code-reviewer: its four blockers are closed. Its one new blocker, an unneeded lint comment, is removed. Its should-fix items are done: an HTML copy over the bucket limit is skipped, not retried for ever, and Gmail never starts on a local database unless asked. That second item is in code, not in `.claude/settings.json`, which is shared config.

- Known issues: admins cannot register brands from a screen yet (INB-004 adds the brand directory); until then every real email is "unassigned" with an ops task. Only INBOX is read (Gmail's Promotions and Updates tabs are part of it): a newsletter Gmail puts in Spam is missed (INB-004 can add a check). A brand without registered sender domains takes any email to its `+alias`. Images, the 600px screenshot (INB-002) and labelling (INB-003) are next. `.env.example` needs the `INBOX_*` names, and `.claude/settings.json` could force `INBOX_TRANSPORT=fake` for sessions like `ADAPTER_MODE` (sessions cannot edit `.env*`; settings are shared config). Competitor send times are stored in UTC.
- Next consumer: INB-002 (images and screenshot) and INB-004 (ops queue and brand directory).
