### 2026-11-07 — GEM-002 — live AI usage, service-role-only metering

- Status: review (stacked on GEM-001 #322; merge after INB-002's migration 090040)
- Branch/worktree: `claude/gem-002-live-ai-usage`
- Commit(s): see the GEM-002 pull request.
- Changed contracts: new service-role-only RPCs `public.reserve_ai_usage(t, operation, feature, estimate)` and `public.settle_ai_usage(t, reservation, actual)`: `ai:` operations only, one reservation per operation (a second reserve is `PT409`), `expires_at = infinity` so an unsettled live call keeps counting until ops settles it. `reserve_fixture_usage` / `reconcile_fixture_usage` now refuse `ai:` operations (as they already refused `email-rewrite:`). `private.reserve_budget` treats `ai:` as live for the cap message and the 80% alert ("real AI requests"). New `public.settle_stale_ai_usage(t, older_than default 1 hour)` (service role or operator) charges live reservations left unsettled at their full estimate, so a crashed call can't block the tenant at cycle renewal. (`private.reconcile_budget` already raises `PT409` since 090011, so it isn't redefined.) `SupabaseBudgetLedger` calls the new RPCs through a typed local signature until the next `pnpm supabase:types`.
- Migrations: `20260914090046_live_ai_usage.sql`.
- Verification run: `vitest run features/ai` 19/19; eslint and tsc clean for `features/ai`. `supabase/tests/live_ai_usage.test.sql` (owner, foreign owner and no-role callers can't reserve, settle, release or pre-seed live usage; the expiry sweep leaves live rows alone; double reserve is PT409; over-estimate settle 23514; second different settle PT409; the stale sweep charges the estimate; privileges). rls-migration-reviewer findings fixed: stray statement fragment, ambiguous `id`, duplicate check now under the tenant lock could not run here (no Docker); CI's Supabase job runs it.
- Known issues: competitor-section labelling (lane B's INB-003) has no workspace, so it needs a platform-level budget: follow-up GEM-003. Regenerate types with `pnpm supabase:types` after merge and drop the local `LiveUsageRpc` cast.
- Next consumer: lane A (BLK-003, ASM-001) can use live Gemini in production once this merges and `GEMINI_API_KEY` is set.

### 2026-11-11 — MEM-001 — agent runtime and shared memory

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/mem-001-agent-runtime`
- Commit(s): see the MEM-001 pull request.
- Changed contracts: new `features/agents/runtime.ts`: an `AgentDefinition` (name, prompt version, purpose, instructions, task, JSON schema + zod output, token and reasoning settings) and `runAgent(def, input, { tenantId, industry, callerKey, memory, log, ai })`. It appends the Brand book (when `usesBrandBook`) and the selected do's and don'ts to the instructions, keeps untrusted content in the task JSON, calls `runAi` through the injected `ai`, validates the answer, and records one run-log entry (agent, version, prompt digest, rule ids, Brand book version, model, cost, verdict, short detail). New `features/agents/memory.ts`: the `MemoryRule` shape, `selectRules` (active, confidence ≥ 0.5, not expired, for this agent; brand first, then industry, then global; max 20), `lessonsBlock`, `brandBookBlock` (truncated at 8,000 chars), `MemoryStore` and in-memory doubles. New AI purpose `plan` → Gemini 2.5 Pro (price added; `GEMINI_PLAN_MODEL`), metered as `playbook`. Lane B's CI-001 asks: `AiProviderRequest.images` (JPEG/PNG/WebP base64, max 8, sent as Gemini `inlineData`; reserved at a fixed 8,000 input tokens per picture instead of its byte size), `AgentDefinition.images`, a `review` purpose (Gemini 2.5 Flash, `GEMINI_REVIEW_MODEL`) for second tries and inspectors, and a per-call `purpose` override in `runAgent`.
- Migrations: `agent_memory` (number chosen at merge, D24): `agent_memory_rules`, `brand_books` (versioned), `agent_runs`; members read their own rows, operators read all, platform and industry rules are never shown to clients; writes only through the service-role commands `add_memory_rule`, `update_memory_rule`, `save_brand_book`, `record_agent_run` (the worker has no direct insert/update/delete either). Runs (AI cost and raw output) are readable by owners and admins only, not staff. `add_memory_rule` needs an `idempotencyKey` and `record_agent_run` a `runKey` (uuid): a retried call returns the same row. `update_memory_rule(rule_id, expected_revision, new_status, new_confidence)` returns the new revision; a stale revision, or turning an expired/retired rule back to active, is `PT409`. New `features/agents/supabase-stores.ts`: `SupabaseMemoryStore` (same selection as the in-memory store; filters built only from validated ids and industry names) and `SupabaseRunLog` (a failed log write never blocks the agent).
- Verification run: `vitest run features/agents features/ai lib/providers`; eslint and tsc clean for the changed files.
- Known issues: generated types need `pnpm supabase:types` after merge; until then the stores use narrow typed signatures. pgTAP `agent_memory.test.sql` runs in CI (no Docker in the supervisor sandbox).
- Next consumer: lanes A and B: define every agent as an `AgentDefinition` and call `runAgent(def, input, { tenantId, industry, callerKey, memory: new SupabaseMemoryStore(db), log: new SupabaseRunLog(db), ai: (i) => runAi(i, { ledger: new SupabaseBudgetLedger(db) }) })` with a service-role `db`. LRN-002 writes rules with `add_memory_rule`; ONB-106 saves the Brand book with `save_brand_book`.

Until part 2, an owner calling `set_fixture_connection` directly can still set a live Klaviyo connection's status (their own workspace only), and a live Klaviyo connection is disconnected only by that command (the card has no Disconnect yet). Before merging, the hosted project must have Supabase Vault (on by default on Supabase; the migration does not create it). The key travels to the database as a command argument; if the hosted database logs statements with their values, it would show there (Supabase does not by default). Nothing is read from Klaviyo after the check yet (part 2: catalog, bestsellers, scheduled campaigns, the Engaged segment). Write scopes are not checked until used.
- Next consumer: under the v3 plan (D19, D22), ONB-102 Catalog analyst (#344), ONB-103 Klaviyo historian (#345) and LRN-001 sync and results (#348) read the key with `klaviyo_api_key`; lane A's push needs Templates and Images.

Brand size from one email is usually `unknown`.

### 2026-11-11 — MEM-002 — thinking room on Gemini 2.5 calls

- Status: review
- Branch/worktree: `claude/mem-002-thinking-room`
- Commit(s): see the MEM-002 pull request.
- Changed contracts: `AiProviderRequest.maxOutputTokens` now means the room for the answer. For Gemini 2.5 with a `reasoningEffort`, the provider adds the thinking budget on top (`geminiOutputTokens`: low +1,024, medium +4,096; capped at 65,536), because 2.5 counts thinking as output and a cut-off inside the thinking came back as `incomplete` (no answer, no retry). The cost reservation in `runAi` uses the same number. 2.5 Pro never gets a budget below 128 (it cannot switch thinking off). Agents keep `maxOutputTokens` at the answer size; lane A's `outputLimit` (EML-001) can go back to the answer size only.
- Migrations: none.
- Verification run: `vitest run lib/providers features/ai features/agents features/strategy features/intelligence` (176 pass); eslint and tsc clean for the changed files.
- Models that decide their own thinking (any 3.x model, and 2.5 Flash or Pro with no `reasoningEffort`) get a fixed 4,096 tokens of thinking room on top of the answer; 2.5 Flash-Lite with no effort does not think and gets none. The total never exceeds 65,536, so an answer room near that cap is trimmed.
- Known issues: none.
- Next consumer: lanes A and B (every AgentDefinition).
